**On these three grounds my own reading is this, and it is not binding:** the sentence leaves illness to the person's own share and healing to God. **It is not an attribution of fault but an attribution of cause** — the sentence "I fell ill" is not a confession of sin but a note of which side the event came from.

**And I state the limits of this reading plainly:**

| What the verse does not say | Why it does not say it |
|---|---|
| **"Illness comes from a person's sin"** | Sin does not occur in the verse; the verb *mariḍtu* is neutral |
| **"God does not give illness"** | The verse does not deny the attribution, it merely does not make it |
| **"Healing comes only by prayer"** | The verse says nothing of secondary causes |

**The explanations transmitted for this verse in the classical commentaries look in the same direction, and I give them in a table:**

| Explanation | Ground | Its weak side |
|---|---|---|
| **What is unpleasant is not attributed to God, out of propriety** | There are similar instances in the Qurʾān: the speech of the jinn, *wa annā lā nadrī a-sharrun urīda bi-man fi'l-arḍi am arāda bihim rabbuhum rashadā* (al-Jinn 72/10 — for the evil no agent is named, for the good one is); this was treated in `072-cin.md` | One verse later there is the attribution *yumītunī* (He causes me to die); death too is unpleasant |
| **The cause belongs to the person, the outcome to God** | Illness comes by causes, healing is beyond the causes | The verse says nothing of causes |
| **Because the sentence is in the conditional mould, the verb is necessarily first person** | The *idhā* condition announces a state; there is no other way of saying "when I fall ill" | The mould itself was chosen; *alladhī yumriḍunī* could also have been said |

**The three explanations do not exclude one another.** And the third completes the first and the second: **the mould was chosen, and the chosen mould changed the attribution.**

The root **م-ر-ض**: **the breakdown of soundness, departure from the mean.** The lexicographers describe the root as **"a defect and weakness occurring in the body or in one's affairs".** The Qurʾān uses the same root of the heart as well: *fī qulūbihim maraḍun* (al-Baqara 2/10). **The word, then, does not belong to the body alone.**

The root **ش-ف-ي**: the core the lexicographers give is **to come to the edge, the outermost point of a thing** (*shafā* — edge; *shafa* — lip). ***Shifāʾ*** is to come to the **edge** of an illness, to come out of it. **Healing, then, is not the annihilation of the illness but the reaching of its boundary.**

**And the mould *fa-huwa yashfīn* must be recorded:** it is exactly the same construction as *fa-huwa yahdīn* in the first sentence. **The *fāʾ* of cause is at work here too: "I fell ill, and therefore He heals."**

### The fourth sentence — وَٱلَّذِى يُمِيتُنِى ثُمَّ يُحْيِينِ

**Two verbs, with *thumma* between them.**

**ثُمَّ** — in Arabic it announces **sequence with an interval (*tarākhī*)**; *fāʾ* is for succession without interval, *thumma* for succession with time in between. **That there is a distance between death and resurrection is thus in the particle itself.**

**And the separating pronoun is absent here.** The first three sentences had *huwa*; here and in the fifth it is absent. **I record this as an observation about the structure and propose an explanation, which is not binding:** the first three sentences are works **seen in this world**, and there the emphasis "He alone" answers a dispute — because feeding, giving drink and healing can look as though they have other agents. **On death and resurrection, however, there is no rival claim to agency to begin with.**

**And the same pair occurs once more in Abraham's mouth in the Qurʾān:** *rabbiya'lladhī yuḥyī wa yumīt* (al-Baqara 2/258) — and there the answer of the one addressed is *anā uḥyī wa umīt*. **There, that is, a rival claim exists; here it does not.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

### The fifth sentence — وَٱلَّذِىٓ أَطْمَعُ أَن يَغْفِرَ لِى خَطِيٓـَٔتِى يَوْمَ ٱلدِّينِ

**The end of the sequence, and the tone changes: from certainty to hope.**

**In four sentences the verbs were declarative: He creates, He feeds, He heals, He causes to die.** In the fifth there is a verb of hope: ***aṭmaʿu*** — "I hope".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verb itself:** Abraham does not state forgiveness as a right or a certainty. **The one uncertain sentence in the sequence is the one concerning his own end.**

**The root ط-م-ع** was analysed in the fifty-first verse and compared there with the sentence of the sorcerers; **I do not repeat it.** The table there showed this: the two sentences are almost exactly the same, their only difference being singular and plural.

**خَطِيٓـَٔة** — root **خ-ط-ء**: **to miss the target, to fail to hit.** Its opposite is *ṣawāb* (hitting the mark). **In the core of the word there is no intent; for what is intentional *ithm* or *dhanb* is used.**

**And a point of word order: the word is singular — *khaṭīʾatī*, "my fault".** Not the plural (*khaṭāyā*). **The sorcerers had used the plural: *khaṭāyānā* (51).**

**The transmitted explanation: the singular announces the class — "whatever fault of mine there may be".** I make no firm choice.

**يَوْمَ ٱلدِّين** — "the day of *dīn*", that is, **the day of reckoning**. The root **د-ي-ن** was analysed in `001-fatiha.md` (*māliki yawmi'd-dīn*) and I do not repeat it. The note there: in the core of the root lie **debt, requital and submission**; *dayn* (debt) and *dīn* (religion) are from the same root.

### The sequence as a whole — an observation

**What the five sentences have in common is that not one of them contains an attribute.** Abraham does not describe his Lord as "great", "powerful" or "wise". **All five of the five sentences are verbal sentences.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the seventy-second verse:** Abraham had asked his people what the idols do — *do they hear, do they benefit, do they harm.* **He had asked about three verbs.** Now he recounts his own side **with verbs** too: He creates, He guides, He feeds, He gives drink, He heals, He causes to die, He brings to life, He forgives. **Eight verbs.**

**The comparison, that is, runs from beginning to end on the same measure: what does it do?**

### What this says to the present

The most concrete side of the sequence is the space the sentence about illness leaves open. **The verse does not tie illness to a punishment; nor does it place healing outside the order of causes.** It stands away from both extremes: neither "what befell you is from your guilt" nor "taking hold of the means is unnecessary".

**What the sentence leaves is where responsibility and surrender part:** a person takes on his own share, he does not take on the outcome. **And this is the same measure as in the third verse of the sūra** — the messenger was told "do not grieve", because it was not he who produced the outcome.

---

## 26/83-89 — رَبِّ هَبْ لِى حُكْمًا وَأَلْحِقْنِى بِٱلصَّٰلِحِينَ · وَٱجْعَل لِّى لِسَانَ صِدْقٍ فِى ٱلْءَاخِرِينَ · وَٱجْعَلْنِى مِن وَرَثَةِ جَنَّةِ ٱلنَّعِيمِ · وَٱغْفِرْ لِأَبِىٓ إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ مِنَ ٱلضَّآلِّينَ · وَلَا تُخْزِنِى يَوْمَ يُبْعَثُونَ · يَوْمَ لَا يَنفَعُ مَالٌ وَلَا بَنُونَ · إِلَّا مَنْ أَتَى ٱللَّهَ بِقَلْبٍ سَلِيمٍ

*Rabbi hab lī ḥukman wa alḥiqnī bi'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn · Wa'jʿal lī lisāna ṣidqin fi'l-ākhirīn · Wa'jʿalnī min warathati jannati'n-naʿīm · Wa'ghfir li-abī innahu kāna mina'ḍ-ḍāllīn · Wa lā tukhzinī yawma yubʿathūn · Yawma lā yanfaʿu mālun wa lā banūn · Illā man ata'llāha bi-qalbin salīm*

"'**My Lord! Bestow judgement upon me and join me to the righteous. · Grant me a tongue of truth among those who come after. · Make me one of the inheritors of the garden of bliss. · And forgive my father; he was one of those who had strayed. · And do not disgrace me on the day when they are raised — · the day when neither wealth nor sons will be of use; · none but he who comes to God with a sound heart.**'"

### Six petitions — their arrangement

**After the five-sentence description come six petitions, and these too follow an order:**

| # | Verse | Petition | Its field |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 83 | *Hab lī ḥukman* | **Knowledge** |
| 2 | 83 | *Alḥiqnī bi'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* | **Company** |
| 3 | 84 | *Lisāna ṣidqin fi'l-ākhirīn* | **Those who come after** |
| 4 | 85 | *Min warathati jannati'n-naʿīm* | **The hereafter** |
| 5 | 86 | *Wa'ghfir li-abī* | **His father** |
| 6 | 87 | *Lā tukhzinī yawma yubʿathūn* | **The day of reckoning** |

**Five petitions for himself, one for another.** And that one is the person standing on the other side of the dispute: **his father.**

**I record this as an observation:** in the seventy-seventh verse it was said *"they are an enemy to me"*; in the eighty-sixth forgiveness is asked for one person from within that same people.

### هَبْ لِى حُكْمًا — the link with Moses

**The same word, in the same sūra, two prophets:**

| Verse | Who | Expression | Mood |
|---|---|---|---|
| **21** | **Moses** | *Fa-wahaba lī rabbī **ḥukman*** | **Perfect** — given |
| **83** | **Abraham** | *Rabbi **hab** lī **ḥukman*** | **Imperative** — asked for |

**The same verb (و-ه-ب), the same object (*ḥukm*), two different moods.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**The root analysis of the word was given in the section on 26/20-22 (ح-ك-م — to make firm, to put on the bit); I do not repeat it.**

### لِسَانَ صِدْقٍ فِى ٱلْءَاخِرِين — "a tongue of truth"

**The construction is an idiom in Arabic and cannot be translated word for word.**

**لِسَان** — the tongue (the organ) and **speech, the language spoken.** In the construction the second sense is at work: **to be well spoken of in the mouths of those who come after, to be remembered with truth.**

**صِدْق** — root **ص-د-ق**: **the correspondence of speech with reality, soundness.** The lexicographers record that in the concrete core of the root lie **hardness and durability** (*rumḥun ṣadq* — a stout spear). **True speech, then, is durable speech.**

**The petition, then, is this: "let a true word remain behind me."** Not to be famous, but **to be remembered with truth**.

**And a link can be made with the mould recorded in `037-saffat.md`:** there the seal of the sequence of prophets was *wa taraknā ʿalayhi fi'l-ākhirīn* ("and We left upon him [a remembrance] among those who came after"), and **Abraham had received that seal** (al-Ṣāffāt 37/108).

| Sūra | Expression | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/84** | *Wa'jʿal lī **lisāna ṣidqin fi'l-ākhirīn*** | **Abraham** — a petition |
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/108** | *Wa **taraknā ʿalayhi fi'l-ākhirīn*** | **God** — granted |

**The same adverbial phrase (*fi'l-ākhirīn*), in two sūras, one a request and one a response.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text. **And as my own reading I record this:** when the two sūras are read together the petition and its answer come to stand side by side. That this link is made is not stated in the text.

### وَٱغْفِرْ لِأَبِىٓ — the most disputed petition

**And this petition is a matter the Qurʾān itself takes up separately.**

**Al-Tawba 9/114 touches directly on this event:** *wa mā kāna'stighfāru Ibrāhīma li-abīhi illā ʿan mawʿidatin waʿadahā iyyāhu fa-lammā tabayyana lahu annahu ʿaduwwun li'llāhi tabarraʾa minh* — "Abraham's asking forgiveness for his father was only because of a promise he had made to him; when it became clear to him that he was an enemy to God, he dissociated himself from him."

**The Qurʾān, that is, both transmits this petition and draws its limit.** This is a datum that can be verified from the text and requires no explanation — **the Qurʾān gives the explanation itself.**

**And at al-Mumtaḥina 60/4 the same event is named as an exception** (*illā qawla Ibrāhīma li-abīhi la-astaghfiranna lak*) **and was treated in `060-mumtehine.md`.** I rely on the note there.

**I do not enter into details about Abraham's father that the Qurʾān does not give;** the reports about his name, his trade and his end are not in the Qurʾān.

**And a point of word order must be recorded:** the ground given is *innahu kāna mina'ḍ-ḍāllīn* — "he was one of those who had strayed".

**The root ض-ل-ل** was analysed in the twentieth verse, and it was recorded there that Moses used it of himself (*wa anā mina'ḍ-ḍāllīn*). **The same word thus occurs twice in the sūra: once by Moses of himself, once by Abraham of his father.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

**And the choice of the word is worth recording:** Abraham does not call his father *kāfir* or *ẓālim*. **He uses the mildest word: one who has lost the way.**

### وَلَا تُخْزِنِى — not to be disgraced

The root **خ-ز-ي**: **to be brought to shame, to be humiliated, to have one's face fall.** *Khizy* — disgrace.

**A point of word order:** Abraham does not say "protect me from the punishment" or "admit me to the garden" — he had already asked for the garden in the eighty-fifth verse. **What he asks for here is not escape from punishment but *not to be shamed*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the verb:** *khizy* is not a pain but the loss of a **standing** — to be made small before others. **The petition, then, looks to the social side of the reckoning.**

### يَوْمَ لَا يَنفَعُ مَالٌ وَلَا بَنُون — two supports

**Two things are named, and both are indefinite: *mālun* and *banūn*.**

**In Arabic an indefinite noun after a negative particle negates the whole class: "no wealth whatever, no sons whatever".**

**And the two things chosen must be recorded:** *māl* (wealth) and *banūn* (sons). **The Qurʾān names the two together in many places** — *al-mālu wa'l-banūna zīnatu'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā* (al-Kahf 18/46), *innamā amwālukum wa awlādukum fitna* (al-Taghābun 64/15 — treated in `064-tegabun.md`).

**I record this as an observation:** both are things that provide **power** and **continuance** in a society. Wealth represents present power, sons future continuation. **The verse voids them both in the same sentence.**

**And a link can be made with the section on Hūd in the sūra:** *amaddakum bi-anʿāmin wa **banīn*** (133) — sons are counted among the favours given to the people of Hūd. **The sūra thus names the same thing in one place as a favour and in another as something that brings no benefit.** Not a contradiction; **two different days are being spoken of.**

### إِلَّا مَنْ أَتَى ٱللَّهَ بِقَلْبٍ سَلِيمٍ — the exception itself

**And one of the most often cited sentences of the sūra.**

**A point of word order must be recorded: what is the exception being made from?**

| Reading | What the exception attaches to | Its meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Disjunctive** | *Lā yanfaʿu mālun wa lā banūn* | "Wealth and sons are of no use; **but he who comes with a sound heart is benefited**" |
| **Conjunctive** | Those who will benefit | "Only for him who comes with a sound heart do [his wealth and his sons] benefit" |

**The first reading is the widespread one and fits the flow of the sentence better; the second is also transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**أَتَى ٱللَّهَ بِـ** — the pattern must be recorded. *Atā … bi-* in Arabic means **"to bring"**; not "to come". **So it is not "he who comes to God" but "he who brings to God".**

**And at `037-saffat.md` 37/84 the same construction occurs of the same person:** *idh **jāʾa** rabbahu **bi-qalbin salīm***. **It was treated there, and the note there holds here too:**

| Sūra | Verb | Of whom | Who says it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/84** | *Jāʾa … bi-* | **Abraham** | The narrator |
| **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/89** | *Atā … bi-* | **General** — "whoever" | **Abraham himself** |

**The same construction, the same structure, two different verbs (*jāʾa* and *atā*) and opposite directions:** in al-Ṣāffāt Abraham himself is described, in al-Shuʿarāʾ Abraham sets a measure. **This overlap was already recorded in `037-saffat.md` and I rely on that treatment.**

**سَلِيم** — root **س-ل-م**: **to be sound, to be without defect, to be in safety.** The root was analysed in `050-kaf.md`, in `072-cin.md` and in `002-bakara.md`. From the same root: *salām* (peace), *silm* (peace-making), *islām* (to submit).

**The *faʿīl* pattern in Arabic can carry both an active and a passive sense**, and in `037-saffat.md` the senses transmitted for this word were given in a table. **I do not repeat it;** its summary is this: **without defect, without illness, sound** (the primary sense) or **submitted** (the second sense).

**What is to be added here is an observation about the context, and I record it as my own reading:** the primary sense of the word *salīm* is **freedom from illness**. **And nine verses earlier illness had occurred:** *wa idhā **mariḍtu** fa-huwa yashfīn* (80). **After recounting the illness of the body and its healing, the sūra sets the soundness of the heart as its measure.** That this link is intended is not stated in the text; that the two words come from the same semantic field and stand nine verses apart is a datum of the text.

**What the heart is to be sound from is not said in the verse.** The transmitted explanations: from associating partners, from hypocrisy, from rancour, from love of the world. **There is no limit in the verse; for that reason I do not impose a preference.** What is to be recorded is this: **the measure is not a deed but a state** — and that state is something "brought".

---

## 26/90-95 — وَأُزْلِفَتِ ٱلْجَنَّةُ لِلْمُتَّقِينَ · وَبُرِّزَتِ ٱلْجَحِيمُ لِلْغَاوِينَ · وَقِيلَ لَهُمْ أَيْنَ مَا كُنتُمْ تَعْبُدُونَ · مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ هَلْ يَنصُرُونَكُمْ أَوْ يَنتَصِرُونَ · فَكُبْكِبُوا۟ فِيهَا هُمْ وَٱلْغَاوُۥنَ · وَجُنُودُ إِبْلِيسَ أَجْمَعُونَ

*Wa uzlifati'l-jannatu li'l-muttaqīn · Wa burrizati'l-jaḥīmu li'l-ghāwīn · Wa qīla lahum ayna mā kuntum taʿbudūn · Min dūni'llāh; hal yanṣurūnakum aw yantaṣirūn · Fa-kubkibū fīhā hum wa'l-ghāwūn · Wa junūdu Iblīsa ajmaʿūn*

"**And the garden was brought near to those who guard themselves; · and the blaze was made plain to sight for those gone astray.** · And it was said to them: '**Where are those you used to serve — · apart from God? Do they help you, or help themselves?**' · **Thereupon they were flung headlong into it: they, and those gone astray, · and the hosts of Iblīs, all together.**"

### Two passive verbs, two opposite movements

**The two verses are mirrors of one another, and their verbs differ:**

| Verse | Verb | Form | Sense | For whom |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **90** | *uzlifat* | Form IV, passive | **Was brought near** | *al-muttaqīn* |
| **91** | *burrizat* | Form II, passive | **Was made plain to sight** | *al-ghāwīn* |

**The verbs do not do opposite work; they do two different kinds of work.** The garden is **brought near** (the distance closes); the blaze is **brought out into view** (visibility increases). **One approaches, the other becomes visible.**

**The root ز-ل-ف** was analysed in the sixty-fourth verse (*wa azlafnā thamma'l-ākharīn*), and the link between these two verses was set out in a table there; **I do not repeat it.**

The root **ب-ر-ز**: **the coming out of what was hidden, appearing in the open.** *Bāriza* — what is out in the open; *burūz* — appearing. **Form II (*barraza*) is transitive and intensive: "it was brought out plainly into view."**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference of the two verbs:** the verses recount what each side meets **in the language of perception**. For the one who guards himself what changes is **distance**; for the one gone astray what changes is **sight**.

### ٱلْغَاوِين — root غ-و-ي

**The root was analysed in `053-necm.md` and I do not repeat it.** The description there: **غ-و-ي** — to lose the right direction, to transgress; *ghayy* — waywardness. **And something was recorded there:** the plural of the word, ***ghāwūn***, is used in the Qurʾān for **those who follow the poets** (al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/224).

**The word thus occurs three times in the sūra:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **91** | *Wa burrizati'l-jaḥīmu li'l-**ghāwīn*** | The side to whom the blaze is shown |
| **94** | *Fa-kubkibū fīhā hum wa'l-**ghāwūn*** | Those who are with those flung in |
| **224** | *Wa'sh-shuʿarāʾu yattabiʿuhumu'l-**ghāwūn*** | **Those who follow the poets** |

**This is a repetition that can be verified from the text, and it binds the two ends of the sūra.** The word from the scene of the gathering in the account of Abraham returns in the final paragraph of the sūra. **What the link means is interpretation and will be treated in the section on verse 224.**

**And a comparison recorded in `053-necm.md` attaches here:** in that same sūra it is said *mā ḍalla ṣāḥibukum wa mā **ghawā*** (53/2) — "your companion has neither strayed nor gone astray". **Al-Najm, that is, clears the messenger of *ghayy*, while al-Shuʿarāʾ describes the *ghāwūn*.** The two sūras stand on the two banks of the same root.

### أَيْنَ مَا كُنتُمْ تَعْبُدُونَ — the return of the question

**And the question of the seventieth verse of the sūra returns twenty-two verses later:**

| Verse | Question | Who asks | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| **70** | ***Mā** taʿbudūn* — "**what** do you serve?" | **Abraham** | **In this world** |
| **92** | ***Ayna** mā kuntum taʿbudūn* — "**where** are those you served?" | **(unnamed)** — "it was said" | **At the gathering** |

**The same verb (*taʿbudūn*), two different interrogative particles: *mā* and *ayna*.** One asks after the kind, the other after the place.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the sameness of the verb in the two verses:** the question in this world had gone unanswered — the people had said *bal* and changed the subject (74). **The question at the gathering goes unanswered too:** in the ninety-third verse the questioning continues, and in the ninety-fourth what comes is not an answer but a result.

### هَلْ يَنصُرُونَكُمْ أَوْ يَنتَصِرُونَ — two forms, one root

The root **ن-ص-ر**: **to help, to support.**

| Word | Form | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| *Yanṣurūna-kum* | Form I, transitive | **Do they help you** |
| *Yantaṣirūn* | Form VIII (*iftiʿāl*) | **Can they defend themselves** |

**In Arabic Form VIII adds reflexivity: a person's doing something for himself.** *Intiṣār* — to help oneself, to defend, to avenge.

**The question therefore has two stages, and the second is the heavier:** "if they cannot help you, can they save themselves?"

**The same two-stage structure was present at `035-fatir.md` 35/14 as well** (*lā yasmaʿū … wa law samiʿū ma'stajābū*) **and was treated there.** In both verses the proof is completed at the second stage.

**And the verb *intaṣara* will return in the last verse of the sūra:** *wa'**ntaṣarū** min baʿdi mā ẓulimū* (227). **The same form, the same root — but there in the positive: "and they defended themselves after being wronged."** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text and will be treated in the section on verse 227.

### فَكُبْكِبُوا۟ — a rare verb

**ك-ب-ك-ب** — a four-letter (quadriliteral) root; the reduplicated form of the root ك-ب-ب. **ك-ب-ب**: to overturn face down, to fling headlong.

**The reduplicated pattern (*kabkaba*) must be recorded:** in Arabic the repetition of a letter announces **the repetition of the act**. The lexicographers explain this verb as **"to be thrown down again and again, rolling over"** — not to fall once and stop, but **to tumble over and over**.

**The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** This is a datum that can be verified from the text.

**And the verb is passive: *kubkibū* — "they were flung".** Who flung them is not said. **The verbs in the ninetieth and ninety-first verses were passive too** (*uzlifat*, *burrizat*), as was the one in the ninety-second (*qīla*). **All four verbs of the scene of the gathering, that is, are given without naming an agent.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the scene is recounted not as the works of an agent but as **things that come to pass**.

### هُمْ وَٱلْغَاوُۥنَ وَجُنُودُ إِبْلِيسَ — the list of those flung in

**Three groups are named, and their order is worth recording:**

| Order | Who | Identity |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Hum* | **Those served** — the "those you used to serve" of the ninety-second verse |
| 2 | *Al-ghāwūn* | **Those who served** |
| 3 | *Junūdu Iblīsa ajmaʿūn* | **The hosts of Iblīs** |

**The one served and the one who served go to the same place.** And this is the counterpart of the sentence *"they are an enemy to me"* in the seventy-seventh verse: **what is served does not save the one who serves.**

**جُنُود** — the plural of *jund*: **an army, a gathered force.** The word occurred in `048-fetih.md` and `085-buruc.md`.

**إِبْلِيس** — the origin of the word is disputed; **it is widely said to derive from the root ب-ل-س** (to lose hope, to be struck dumb), and it is also transmitted that it is the arabised form of a foreign word. **I make no firm choice.**

**And who his hosts are is not said in the verse.** I do not enter into a detail the Qurʾān does not give.

---

## 26/96-104 — قَالُوا۟ وَهُمْ فِيهَا يَخْتَصِمُونَ · تَٱللَّهِ إِن كُنَّا لَفِى ضَلَٰلٍ مُّبِينٍ · إِذْ نُسَوِّيكُم بِرَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ · وَمَآ أَضَلَّنَآ إِلَّا ٱلْمُجْرِمُونَ · فَمَا لَنَا مِن شَٰفِعِينَ · وَلَا صَدِيقٍ حَمِيمٍ · فَلَوْ أَنَّ لَنَا كَرَّةً فَنَكُونَ مِنَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ · إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً ۖ وَمَا كَانَ أَكْثَرُهُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ · وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

*Qālū wa hum fīhā yakhtaṣimūn · Ta'llāhi in kunnā la-fī ḍalālin mubīn · Idh nusawwīkum bi-rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Wa mā aḍallanā illa'l-mujrimūn · Fa-mā lanā min shāfiʿīn · Wa lā ṣadīqin ḥamīm · Fa-law anna lanā karratan fa-nakūna mina'l-muʾminīn · Inna fī dhālika la-āyatan wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn · Wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*

"There, quarrelling with one another, they say: · '**By God, we were in plain error — · when we made you equal to the Lord of all the worlds! · It was only the guilty who led us astray. · Now we have no intercessor, · and not one devoted friend. · If only we had another turn, that we might be among the believers!**' · In this there is surely a sign; but most of them were not believers. · And your Lord — He indeed — is the Mighty, the Merciful."

### يَخْتَصِمُون — quarrelling

The root **خ-ص-م**: **to quarrel, to litigate.** Form VIII (*ikhtiṣām*) announces reciprocity: **to quarrel with one another.**

**And at `037-saffat.md` 37/27 the same scene was recorded:** *fa-aqbala baʿḍuhum ʿalā baʿḍin yatasāʾalūn* — "they turn to one another, questioning". **It was treated there, and what was recorded there was this: the same sentence occurs in two different forms, in the fire and in the garden.**

**Al-Shuʿarāʾ's difference: the verb is not *tasāʾul* (questioning) but *ikhtiṣām* (quarrelling).** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

### تَٱللَّهِ — a rare particle of oath

**In Arabic an oath is made with three letters: *wāw*, *bāʾ*, *tāʾ*.** **تَ** is the least used, and it comes **only with the name of God**.

**The lexicographers record that the *tāʾ* oath announces *taʿajjub* (astonishment).** Not "by God", then, but **"by God — it is hardly to be believed!"**

**And the word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān; in each there is a context of astonishment** (Yūsuf 12/73, 12/85, 12/91; al-Naḥl 16/56; al-Anbiyāʾ 21/57).

**I record this as an observation:** the oath sworn in the fire is not to prove a claim — **it announces their astonishment at their own past.**

### إِن كُنَّا لَفِى ضَلَٰلٍ مُّبِين — the lightened *in*

**A grammatical note:** the *in* here is not the negative particle but **the lightened form of *in*** (*in al-mukhaffafa*; its original is *inna*). Its mark is the **lām** at the head of the predicate (*la-fī*). **The sense, then, is not "we were not" but "we were".**

**This is a construction easily confused in Arabic, and a similar case was recorded in `102-tekasur.md`.**

### إِذْ نُسَوِّيكُم بِرَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِين — the word of the confession

The root **س-و-ي**: **to be equal, to make equal, to set straight.** Form II (*sawwā*) is transitive: **to hold equal.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the confession is not "we served you" but "we made you equal".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the verb:** the definition of associating partners is made here not through **worship** but through **equating**. **The matter, that is, is not whether something was worshipped; it is whether something was set in the same pan of the scales as God.**

**And this accords with Abraham's question in the seventy-second verse:** he too had asked not after the content of worship but after **the comparison** (does it hear, does it benefit, does it harm).

### وَمَآ أَضَلَّنَآ إِلَّا ٱلْمُجْرِمُون — the transfer of the guilt

**And immediately after the confession comes the transfer of responsibility.**

| Verse | Sentence | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **97-98** | *In kunnā la-fī ḍalālin mubīn … idh nusawwīkum* | **Confession** — "we did it" |
| **99** | *Wa mā aḍallanā illa'l-mujrimūn* | **Transfer** — "they led us astray" |

**There is no verse between the two; confession and transfer are adjacent.**

**I record this as an observation:** the same mouth says two different things in two sentences. **And the Qurʾān records this scene in other sūras as well** — Sabaʾ 34/31-33, al-Ṣāffāt 37/27-33 (treated in `037-saffat.md`), al-Aḥzāb 33/67. **What all of them have in common is the passing of the guilt to the other side.**

**ٱلْمُجْرِمُون** — root **ج-ر-م**: the concrete sense the lexicographers give is **to pluck fruit from the branch** (*jaramu'n-nakhl* — to harvest dates). **From there it passes to "to earn" and "to earn something evil, to commit a crime".** *Mujrim* — one who earns guilt.

**And that the word is left indefinite must be recorded: who is spoken of is not said.** The forefathers? The leaders? The satans? **The text does not say.**

### فَمَا لَنَا مِن شَٰفِعِينَ وَلَا صَدِيقٍ حَمِيمٍ — two absences

**Two things are sought, and neither is there:**

| Word | Root | What |
|---|---|---|
| *Shāfiʿīn* — **plural** | ش-ف-ع | **Intercessors** — mediators |
| *Ṣadīqin ḥamīm* — **singular** | ص-د-ق / ح-م-م | **A devoted friend** |

**The difference of number must be recorded:** the intercessor is **plural**, the friend **singular**. **The transmitted explanation: intercessors may be many, but a true friend is one; both are said to be absent — "neither a crowd of mediators nor a single friend."**

The root **ش-ف-ع**: **to make a pair, to double what is single.** *Shafʿ* — even (the *al-shafʿi wa'l-watr* of al-Fajr 89/3 — treated in `089-fecr.md`). **Intercession is not leaving a person alone but another's joining himself to his side.** The word, that is, means before "mediation" **"not being alone"**.

**And the work of the sentence is understood from this:** *fa-mā lanā min shāfiʿīn* — "there is no one who joins himself to us." **The loneliness is in the root of the word.**

**حَمِيم** — root **ح-م-م**: **hot, very hot.** From the same root: *ḥamīm* (boiling water — it does not occur in the sūra apart from 26/101, but it is frequent in the Qurʾān) and *ḥamīm* (**an intimate friend**).

**Two senses in the same word, and the link the lexicographers make is this: friendship too is a kind of "heat"** — the warmth of concern and closeness. **The word was treated in `069-hakka.md` and at `070-mearic.md` 70/10 (*wa lā yasʾalu ḥamīmun ḥamīmā*).**

**And this verse was already cited in `078-nebe.md`;** I rely on the note there.

**I record a point about the word:** the same word is used in the Qurʾān both for **the boiling water of the fire** and for **a devoted friend**. **The verse makes those who look for the second speak from inside the first.** I leave this as an observation; I do not claim that the verse intends it.

### فَلَوْ أَنَّ لَنَا كَرَّةً — the wish to return

The root **ك-ر-ر**: **to turn, to repeat, to wheel round in an attack and charge again.** *Karra* — a turn, a repetition.

**لَوْ** here announces **a wish (*tamannī*) whose fulfilment is impossible.** **The apodosis of the sentence is not stated — in Arabic this is called the elision of the *jawāb law*, and it announces that the wish goes unanswered.**

**And the same wish is repeated in many places in the Qurʾān:** *wa law tarā idh wuqifū ʿala'n-nāri fa-qālū yā laytanā nuraddu* (al-Anʿām 6/27), *rabbanā akhrijnā naʿmal ṣāliḥan ghayra'lladhī kunnā naʿmal* (Fāṭir 35/37 — treated in `035-fatir.md`). **What all of them have in common is that the wish goes unanswered.**

**And a point of word order: the wish is not "let us enter the garden" but "let us be *among the believers*".** *Fa-nakūna **mina'l-muʾminīn***. **What is asked for, that is, is not an outcome but a standing.**

**And this is the same word as in the seal of the sūra: *wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn*.** The scene of the gathering, which runs from the ninety-second verse to the hundred and second, ends by arriving at the word of the seal. **This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it shows why the seal is set exactly here.**

### The sealing of the account — 103-104

**The third seal of the sūra. The mould was treated at 26/8-9; I do not repeat it.**

**An observation about position:** the account of Abraham ends without recounting the worldly fate of his people. **The seal is set not after a destruction but after a scene of the gathering.** Among the seven accounts this is so only here.

---

## The shared frame of the five accounts — 26/105-191 (introduction)

**From the hundred and fifth verse to the hundred and ninety-first there are five accounts, and all five sit in the same mould.** The whole of the mould was given in five tables in the section **"The backbone of the sūra"**. **I do not repeat it here;** in the verse headings below only the parts **peculiar to each account** will be treated.

**The word analyses of the shared mould are made once here and will not be repeated in the four accounts that follow.**

### كَذَّبَتْ … ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ — the point of the plural

**All five of the five accounts open with this sentence, and in all five the object is plural: *al-mursalīn* — "those sent".**

**But to each people one messenger came.** The people of Noah denied Noah, ʿĀd denied Hūd, Thamūd denied Ṣāliḥ. **The text, however, says "they denied the messengers".**

**The transmitted explanations:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| **To deny one messenger is to deny them all** | The essential message the messengers bring is one |
| **They had denied the messengers before them too** | Each people had received news of the one before |
| **The class is meant** | *Al-mursalīn* is a definite plural; it announces the institution of messengership |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** What is to be recorded is this: **the sentence ties a single event to a whole attitude.** And the entire structure of the sūra is built on this — **the same mould is repeated seven times, because what is recounted is not seven separate events but one attitude.**

### إِنِّى لَكُمْ رَسُولٌ أَمِينٌ — the claim of trustworthiness

The root **أ-م-ن**: **to be safe, to be at ease in heart.** From the same root: *īmān* (to trust, to believe), *amāna*, *amn*, *amīn*.

**And a point of word order must be recorded: *lakum* has been brought forward.** The ordinary order would have been *innī rasūlun amīnun lakum*. **In Arabic bringing forward (*taqdīm*) announces specification and concern: "for you", "belonging to you".**

**The messenger, that is, introduces himself in relation to those he addresses:** "I am a trustworthy messenger sent **to you**."

**And the attribute *amīn* must be recorded:** the messenger does not introduce himself as "learned", "superior" or "chosen". **He introduces himself as trustworthy** — that is, with an attribute those he addresses can test by their own experience.

**And the same attribute will be used of the one who brings the revelation in verse 193 of the sūra:** *nazala bihi'r-rūḥu'l-**amīn***. **In the sūra *amīn* thus occurs in two places: of the five messengers and of the one who brings down the revelation.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text and will be treated in the section on 192-199.

### فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ — two commands

The root **ت-ق-ي** (the original root **و-ق-ي**): **to guard, to take shelter.** *Taqwā* is not "to fear" but **"to put oneself under protection"**. The root was analysed in `002-bakara.md`, in `049-hucurat.md` and in `092-leyl.md`.

The root **ط-و-ع**: **to obey willingly.** *Ṭawʿ* — willingness; its opposite is *karh* (compulsion). The root was treated at `041-fussilet.md` 41/11 (*ataynā ṭāʾiʿīn*).

**And the order of the two commands must be recorded: first guarding oneself against God, then obeying the messenger.** The messenger asks to be obeyed on a ground that first attaches to God.

**And the elision in *aṭīʿūn*:** its original is *aṭīʿūnī*; the final *yāʾ* has dropped for the sake of the verse-ending. **This is a kind of elision frequently repeated in the sūra** — *yukadhdhibūn(i)*, *yaqtulūn(i)*, *yahdīn(i)*, *yasqīn(i)*, *yashfīn(i)*, *yuḥyīn(i)*. **The verse-ending scheme of the sūra, that is, drops the pronouns at the ends of verbs.** This is a fact that can be verified from the text and bears directly on the sūra's single-voiced structure.

### وَمَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَجْرٍ — the refusal of a wage

**The five occurrences of the sentence and its function within the sūra were tabulated in the section "The backbone of the sūra"; I do not repeat it.**

**What is to be added here are two grammatical notes.**

**One — *min ajrin*.** The *min* that comes after a negative particle announces **comprehensiveness (*istighrāq*)** in Arabic: "no wage whatever". **Not "I ask for little", then, but "I ask for none".**

**Two — *in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*.** The *in* here is a negative particle (in the sense of *mā*), and together with *illā* it sets up **restriction (*qaṣr*)**: "my wage falls **only** upon the Lord of all the worlds."

**And the preposition *ʿalā* must be recorded:** in Arabic *ʿalā* announces **debt and obligation** (*ʿalayhi daynun* — he has a debt upon him). **The wage, that is, is recounted not as a favour but as an undertaking.**

---

## 26/105-110 — كَذَّبَتْ قَوْمُ نُوحٍ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · إِذْ قَالَ لَهُمْ أَخُوهُمْ نُوحٌ أَلَا تَتَّقُونَ · إِنِّى لَكُمْ رَسُولٌ أَمِينٌ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَمَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَجْرٍ ۖ إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ

*Kadhdhabat qawmu Nūḥin'il-mursalīn · Idh qāla lahum akhūhum Nūḥun alā tattaqūn · Innī lakum rasūlun amīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin; in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn*

"**The people of Noah too denied the messengers.** · When their brother Noah said to them: '**Will you not guard yourselves? · I am a trustworthy messenger sent to you. · So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · I ask you no wage for it; my wage falls only upon the Lord of all the worlds. · So guard yourselves against God and obey me.**'"

### The shared frame

**Five of these six verses are the shared mould of the sūra and were treated in the section above. I do not repeat it.**

**The only thing peculiar to the account of Noah is that the mould is set up here for the first time** — the four accounts that follow are its repetition.

**And the second occurrence of the sentence *fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn* (110) comes here with only one verse between.** What this means was treated in Table 4 of the section "The backbone of the sūra".

### أَخُوهُمْ نُوحٌ — "their brother"

**The word occurs in four of the five accounts (106, 124, 142, 161) and drops with Shuʿayb (177). The reason was treated in the section on the backbone; I do not repeat it.**

**What is to be added here is a note on sense: *akh* here announces not brotherhood of lineage but *fellowship of people*.** In Arabic one of a community is called "their brother" in relation to that community.

**And this is a recurring note in the Qurʾān's understanding of messengership:** the messenger is from within the community he is sent to. **There are two exceptions within the sūra itself:** Moses (who goes to the people of Pharaoh from outside) and Shuʿayb (whose addressees are named by a place).

### The place of the account of Noah within the Qurʾān

**The account of Noah occurs in many sūras of the Qurʾān, and in this commentary the section `071-nuh.md` was given over to it entirely.** I do not repeat the findings there.

**The part that falls to al-Shuʿarāʾ must be recorded:** the sūra does not recount Noah's method of calling (in secret and openly, by night and by day — Nūḥ 71/5-9); **it does not recount the ark, the flood, his son, or the length of time either.**

| Sūra | What it brings to the front | Where it was treated |
|---|---|---|
| **Nūḥ 71** | The method of calling, the length of time, the prayer | `071-nuh.md` |
| **Hūd 11/25-49** | The ark, his son, the flood | — |
| **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/105-122** | **The objection "the lowest have followed you"** | here |
| **Al-Ṣāffāt 37/75-82** | The deliverance and his progeny | `037-saffat.md` |
| **Al-Qamar 54/9-16** | The flood itself | `054-kamer.md` |

**Al-Shuʿarāʾ, that is, takes from the account of Noah only one dispute: the question of who has believed.** This suits the sūra as a whole — the sūra recounts not events but **speeches**.

---

## 26/111-115 — قَالُوٓا۟ أَنُؤْمِنُ لَكَ وَٱتَّبَعَكَ ٱلْأَرْذَلُونَ · قَالَ وَمَا عِلْمِى بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ · إِنْ حِسَابُهُمْ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ رَبِّى ۖ لَوْ تَشْعُرُونَ · وَمَآ أَنَا۠ بِطَارِدِ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ · إِنْ أَنَا۠ إِلَّا نَذِيرٌ مُّبِينٌ

*Qālū a-nuʾminu laka wa'ttabaʿaka'l-ardhalūn · Qāla wa mā ʿilmī bimā kānū yaʿmalūn · In ḥisābuhum illā ʿalā rabbī law tashʿurūn · Wa mā anā bi-ṭāridi'l-muʾminīn · In anā illā nadhīrun mubīn*

"They said: '**Shall we believe you when the lowest of the people have followed you?**' · He said: '**I have no knowledge of what they have been doing. · Their reckoning falls only upon my Lord — if only you understood! · I am not one to drive away the believers. · I am nothing but a clear warner.**'"

### The kind of the objection

**The people do not discuss what Noah has said.** They do not speak of God's oneness, of the hereafter, of messengership. **Their objection concerns who has believed.**

**I record this as an observation:** the five answers the other side gives in the sūra differ from one another, and in none of them is there a counter-proof. **This is the first answer, and its subject is not the content but the composition of the parties.**

### ٱلْأَرْذَلُون — the weight of the word

The root **ر-ذ-ل**: **the worthless, the low, the useless part of a thing.** *Ardhal* — an elative: **the lowest, the most worthless.** The Qurʾān uses the same word of the feeblest stage of life as well: *ardhali'l-ʿumur* (al-Naḥl 16/70; al-Ḥajj 22/5).

**And that the word has a social sense is clear from the telling of the same event at Hūd 11/27:** *wa mā narāka'ttabaʿaka illa'lladhīna hum arādhilunā **bādiya'r-raʾy*** — "we see none following you but our lowest, at first glance". **The objection, that is, looks to economic and social standing.**

**I record this as an observation, and it belongs to the same family as the objection recorded at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/31:** *law lā nuzzila hādha'l-qurʾānu ʿalā rajulin mina'l-qaryatayni ʿaẓīm* — "if only this Qurʾān had been sent down upon a great man of the two towns". **Both objections make the standing of those who carry or receive the message the measure.**

### أَنُؤْمِنُ لَكَ — *li-*, not *bi-*

**In the forty-ninth verse Pharaoh too had used the same mould: *āmantum lahu*.**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **49** | **Pharaoh** | *Āmantum **lahu** qabla an ādhana lakum* |
| **111** | **The people of Noah** | *A-nuʾminu **laka*** |

**In both places *āmana li-* is used: "to trust you, to hold you true".** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text. **And in both the subject is not what is to be believed but the *person* to be believed.**

### وَمَا عِلْمِى بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ — the limit of the answer

**Noah does not defend those who have believed.** He does not say "they are good people". **He says "I do not know what they have been doing".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the sentence itself:** the answer rejects the ground the objection rests on. **The objection made the quality of the believers the measure; the answer says that measure does not belong to the messenger.**

**And the next verse closes this plainly: *in ḥisābuhum illā ʿalā rabbī* — "their reckoning falls only upon my Lord".**

**And the preposition *ʿalā* must be recorded:** the same preposition was in the sentence about the wage (*ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*). **In both sentences *ʿalā* ties the obligation to God: the wage and the reckoning both belong to Him.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**لَوْ تَشْعُرُونَ** — "if only you understood". The root **ش-ع-ر**: **to perceive what is fine and hidden.** From the same root come *shiʿr* (poetry) and *shāʿir* (poet) — the word will return in the final section of the sūra (224). **This is a repetition that can be verified from the text, and the analysis of the root will be made there.**

### وَمَآ أَنَا۠ بِطَارِدِ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِين — refusing to drive away

The root **ط-ر-د**: **to send away, to drive off, to expel.**

**And the word order of the sentence must be recorded: the *mā … bi-* mould.** In Arabic the *bāʾ* that comes on the predicate of the negative *mā* **reinforces the negation**: "I will never drive them away."

**And the object must be recorded: *al-muʾminīn* — "the believers".** The people had called them *al-ardhalūn* ("the lowest"); **Noah names those same persons *al-muʾminīn*.**

| Who | The naming | Measure |
|---|---|---|
| **The people** | *Al-ardhalūn* | **Social standing** |
| **Noah** | *Al-muʾminīn* | **Faith** |

**The same people, two names.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And the Qurʾān takes up this matter elsewhere too:** *wa lā taṭrudi'lladhīna yadʿūna rabbahum bi'l-ghadāti wa'l-ʿashiyy* (al-Anʿām 6/52) — the same root (*taṭrud*), the same prohibition. **And Sūrat ʿAbasa treats another face of this matter; it was treated in `080-abese.md`.**

### إِنْ أَنَا۠ إِلَّا نَذِيرٌ مُّبِين — the description of the task

**The mould of restriction (*qaṣr*): *in … illā*.** "I am nothing but a clear warner."

**And it ties to the third verse of the sūra. I record it as my own reading:** at 26/3 the messenger was told *"you are almost destroying yourself"* — that is, he was informed that he is not responsible for the outcome. **In the hundred and fifteenth verse another messenger states the same limit with his own mouth: "I am only a warner."**

**And the list of limits tabulated at `028-kasas.md` 28/56 is a relative of this verse as well.** I rely on the note there.

**مُّبِين** — a word occurring five times in the sūra; its analysis was made and tabulated at 26/1-2. **That it qualifies *nadhīr* here must be recorded: the warning is not hidden or veiled — it is a warning that *makes clear*.**

---

## 26/116-122 — قَالُوا۟ لَئِن لَّمْ تَنتَهِ يَٰنُوحُ لَتَكُونَنَّ مِنَ ٱلْمَرْجُومِينَ · قَالَ رَبِّ إِنَّ قَوْمِى كَذَّبُونِ · فَٱفْتَحْ بَيْنِى وَبَيْنَهُمْ فَتْحًا وَنَجِّنِى وَمَن مَّعِىَ مِنَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ · فَأَنجَيْنَٰهُ وَمَن مَّعَهُۥ فِى ٱلْفُلْكِ ٱلْمَشْحُونِ · ثُمَّ أَغْرَقْنَا بَعْدُ ٱلْبَاقِينَ · إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً ۖ وَمَا كَانَ أَكْثَرُهُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ · وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

*Qālū la-in lam tantahi yā Nūḥu la-takūnanna mina'l-marjūmīn · Qāla rabbi inna qawmī kadhdhabūn · Fa'ftaḥ baynī wa baynahum fatḥan wa najjinī wa man maʿiya mina'l-muʾminīn · Fa-anjaynāhu wa man maʿahu fi'l-fulki'l-mashḥūn · Thumma aghraqnā baʿdu'l-bāqīn · Inna fī dhālika la-āyatan wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn · Wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*

"They said: '**Noah! If you do not stop, you will be among those stoned!**' · He said: '**My Lord! My people have denied me. · Open a clear opening between me and them; and save me and the believers who are with me.**' · **So We saved him and those with him in the laden ark; · then We drowned the rest who remained.** · In this there is surely a sign; but most of them were not believers. · And your Lord — He indeed — is the Mighty, the Merciful."

### The mould of the threat

**And a mould of threat that is repeated twice in the sūra occurs here for the first time:**

| Verse | Who | Expression | The threat |
|---|---|---|---|
| **116** | **The people of Noah** | *La-in lam tantahi yā Nūḥu la-takūnanna mina'l-**marjūmīn*** | **To be stoned** |
| **167** | **The people of Lot** | *La-in lam tantahi yā Lūṭu la-takūnanna mina'l-**mukhrajīn*** | **To be driven out** |

**The two sentences are the same but for one word.** This is a datum that can be verified from the text and another instance of the sūra's technique of repetition.

The root **ن-ه-ي**: **an end, an extremity, the place where a thing finishes** (*muntahā* — the final point reached). *Intahā* — to desist, to make an end. **Not "be silent", then, but "finish with it".**

The root **ر-ج-م**: **to stone, to throw stones.** The word is used in the Qurʾān both for stoning in deed and for stoning with words (cursing, driving off). **Another use of the root (the stoning of the satans) was treated at `067-mulk.md` 67/5 and in `081-tekvir.md`.**

### فَٱفْتَحْ بَيْنِى وَبَيْنَهُمْ فَتْحًا — "give judgement"

The root **ف-ت-ح**: **to open.** And the lexicographers record a second semantic field of the root: **to give judgement, to settle a case.** From the same root: *fattāḥ* (the one who judges — the *al-Fattāḥu'l-ʿAlīm* of Sabaʾ 34/26).

**The link the lexicographers make is this: a judgement opens a matter that was closed.** The case, that is, stands "closed", and the judge "opens" it.

**The root was analysed in `048-fetih.md` and I do not repeat it.**

**And the cognate accusative must be recorded: *fa'ftaḥ … fatḥan*.** The verb is reinforced with a verbal noun from its own root. **In Arabic this construction strengthens the verb: "open a decisive opening", that is, "give a decisive judgement."**

**And the word order of the request must be recorded:** Noah does not ask for destruction. **He says "open between us"** — he asks, that is, for separation and judgement. **And immediately after he asks for deliverance: *wa najjinī wa man maʿiya mina'l-muʾminīn*.**

**In `071-nuh.md` a prayer of Noah's different from the one in this sūra was treated** (*rabbi lā tadhar ʿala'l-arḍi mina'l-kāfirīna dayyārā* — Nūḥ 71/26). **The difference between the two prayers is worth recording:**

| Sūra | Prayer | Its content |
|---|---|---|
| **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/118** | *Fa'ftaḥ baynī wa baynahum fatḥan* | **Judgement and deliverance** |
| **Nūḥ 71/26** | *Lā tadhar ʿala'l-arḍi mina'l-kāfirīna dayyārā* | **Destruction** |

**This is a difference that can be verified from the text.** In what order the two prayers were made is not said in the Qurʾān; **I set up no ordering.**

### ٱلْفُلْكِ ٱلْمَشْحُون — the laden ark

The root **ش-ح-ن**: **to fill, to load.** *Mashḥūn* — a passive participle: **filled, laden.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the building of the ark is not recounted.** The sūra names the ark for the first and only time **already laden**. **The sūra, that is, gives not the process but the result.**

**And the same attribute occurs once more in the Qurʾān:** *wa āyatun lahum annā ḥamalnā dhurriyyatahum fi'l-fulki'l-**mashḥūn*** (Yā Sīn 36/41 — treated in `036-yasin.md`). **The same construction, in two sūras.**

### ثُمَّ أَغْرَقْنَا بَعْدُ ٱلْبَاقِين — the same order again

**And the arrangement is the same as in the account of Moses: first the saving (119), then the drowning (120).**

| Account | Saving | Destruction |
|---|---|---|
| **Moses** | **65** — *wa anjaynā Mūsā wa man maʿahu* | **66** — *thumma aghraqna'l-ākharīn* |
| **Noah** | **119** — *fa-anjaynāhu wa man maʿahu* | **120** — *thumma aghraqnā baʿdu'l-bāqīn* |

**In both accounts the same verb (*anjā*), the same conjunction (*thumma*), the same verb (*aghraqa*).** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**The difference is in one word:** in the account of Moses the drowned are *al-ākharīn* ("the others"), in the account of Noah *al-bāqīn* ("those who remained"). **The second word announces that no one was left apart from those saved.**

**بَعْدُ** — "afterwards, thereafter". It comes as an adverb with the following genitive elided (*baʿdu*), that is, "after that".

### The sealing of the account — 121-122

**The fourth seal of the sūra. The mould was treated at 26/8-9; I do not repeat it.**

---

## 26/123-127 — كَذَّبَتْ عَادٌ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · إِذْ قَالَ لَهُمْ أَخُوهُمْ هُودٌ أَلَا تَتَّقُونَ · إِنِّى لَكُمْ رَسُولٌ أَمِينٌ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَمَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَجْرٍ ۖ إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Kadhdhabat ʿĀdun'il-mursalīn · Idh qāla lahum akhūhum Hūdun alā tattaqūn · Innī lakum rasūlun amīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin; in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*

"**ʿĀd too denied the messengers.** · When their brother Hūd said to them: '**Will you not guard yourselves? · I am a trustworthy messenger sent to you. · So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · I ask you no wage for it; my wage falls only upon the Lord of all the worlds.**'"

### The shared frame — tabulated above

**All five of the five verses are the shared mould of the sūra; the word analyses were made in the introductory section on 26/105-191 and are not repeated.**

**The only difference from the account of Noah is the name of those addressed: *ʿĀd* in place of *qawmu Nūḥ*, *Hūd* in place of *Nūḥ*.**

### عَاد and the place of the people of Hūd

**ʿĀd is one of the two peoples the Qurʾān names as belonging to the Arabian peninsula (the other is Thamūd).** The Qurʾān associates them with the region of **al-Aḥqāf** (al-Aḥqāf 46/21 — treated in `046-ahkaf.md`) and names **Iram** (al-Fajr 89/6-8 — treated in `089-fecr.md`).

**What was recorded in `089-fecr.md` holds here too and I do not repeat it:** Sūrat al-Fajr names ʿĀd as *irama dhāti'l-ʿimād* ("Iram of the pillars") and says *allatī lam yukhlaq mithluhā fi'l-bilād* ("the like of which was not created in the lands").

**And the ʿĀd section of al-Shuʿarāʾ treats exactly this: buildings.** The hundred and twenty-eighth and hundred and twenty-ninth verses are the counterpart of the note about *ʿimād* ("pillars") in al-Fajr. **This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.**

**And the manner of ʿĀd's destruction (the wind) was treated at `054-kamer.md` 54/18-21 and at `069-hakka.md` 69/6-8.** **Al-Shuʿarāʾ does not say the manner of the destruction** — it says only *fa-ahlaknāhum* (139). This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

---

## 26/128-130 — أَتَبْنُونَ بِكُلِّ رِيعٍ ءَايَةً تَعْبَثُونَ · وَتَتَّخِذُونَ مَصَانِعَ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَخْلُدُونَ · وَإِذَا بَطَشْتُم بَطَشْتُمْ جَبَّارِينَ

*A-tabnūna bi-kulli rīʿin āyatan taʿbathūn · Wa tattakhidhūna maṣāniʿa laʿallakum takhludūn · Wa idhā baṭashtum baṭashtum jabbārīn*

"**Do you build a monument on every high place, to no purpose? · And take for yourselves strongholds, as though you would abide for ever? · And when you seize, you seize like tyrants!**"

### Three verses, three offences

**The most detailed list of offences a prophet directs at his people in the sūra is here.** And the structure of the list must be recorded:

| Verse | Verb | Field | Mould |
|---|---|---|---|
| **128** | *Tabnūna* — building | **The monument** | **Question** (*a-*) |
| **129** | *Tattakhidhūna* — taking | **The structure** | Attached to the question |
| **130** | *Baṭashtum* — seizing | **The use of force** | **Statement** (a conditional sentence) |

**The first two are questions, the third a judgement.** And the first two concern **making**, the third **behaving**.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the arrangement of the three verses:** the list begins with stone and arrives at the human being. **First what they build, then how they behave.** And all three look to the same core: **the claim to permanence.**

### رِيع — a rare word

The root **ر-ي-ع**: the senses the lexicographers give are **to rise, to increase, to multiply**. The renderings transmitted for *rīʿ*:

| Rendering | Ground |
|---|---|
| **A high hill, a rise beside a road** | The most widely transmitted sense |
| **A mountain pass, a fork in the road** | *Rīʿ* — the rising part of a road |
| **A high place, a ridge** | The root's core of "rising" |

**The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** This is a datum that can be verified from the text.

**And *bi-kulli* must be recorded: "on every".** What is spoken of, that is, is not a single structure but **a widespread practice**: one on every rise.

### ءَايَة — what it means here

**The word occurs eight times in the sūra in the seal (*inna fī dhālika la-āyatan*) and had occurred in the fourth verse as well (*mina's-samāʾi āyatan*).**

**Here its sense is different, and the rendering the lexicographers give is: a mark, a sign, a landmark.** This is already the core of the root (**أ-ي-ي**): **a sign that makes a thing known.**

**So *āyatan* here means not "a miracle" but "**a marker set up**" — a structure built to be seen from afar.**

**And this is one of the most striking repetitions of a word in the sūra. I record it as my own reading:** at the end of every account the sūra says *"in this there is a **sign**"*. **The people of ʿĀd, meanwhile, erect their own *signs*** — one on every hill. **One side sets up a sign, the other side does not see the sign that has been left.** That this link is intended is not stated in the text; the two uses of the word are a datum of the text.

### تَعْبَثُونَ — idle work

The root **ع-ب-ث**: **to do a thing without purpose or use; to trifle.** *ʿAbath* — idle work, aimless occupation. The Qurʾān negates the same root of creation: *a-fa-ḥasibtum annamā khalaqnākum **ʿabathā*** (al-Muʾminūn 23/115).

**A point of word order:** the verb *taʿbathūn* stands in the sentence as **a circumstantial clause** — "doing it to no purpose", "trifling". The offence, that is, is not the erecting of a structure; **it is the manner of the erecting.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the position of the word in the sentence:** the verse does not forbid building. **It criticises purposelessness.** *ʿAbath* is not a thing's having no use but **the doer's regarding no purpose**.

### مَصَانِع — strongholds or cisterns

The root **ص-ن-ع**: **to make, to manufacture, to work with skill.** The plural of *maṣnaʿ*, *maṣāniʿ*: **a thing made, a work of manufacture.**

**The renderings transmitted for the word:**

| Rendering | Ground |
|---|---|
| **Solid structures, strongholds, palaces** | The root's sense of "making with skill" |
| **Water cisterns, reservoirs** | In Arabic the word *maṣnaʿ* is used for a structure in which water is stored as well |
| **Large works of manufacture generally** | The widest sense of the root |

**I do not impose a preference.** Both renderings accord with the continuation of the verse, because both are things made for **permanence**: a stronghold and a cistern are alike structures directed at the future.

**The word occurs in this pattern only here in the Qurʾān.**

### لَعَلَّكُمْ تَخْلُدُونَ — the claim to everlastingness

The root **خ-ل-د**: **to be lasting, to continue on.** It is used often in the Qurʾān of the garden and of the fire (*khālidīna fīhā*).

**And *laʿalla* must be recorded:** in the third verse (*laʿallaka bākhiʿun*) it was recorded that this particle announces **not expectation but anxiety**. **Here its function is different: it announces mockery or intent.** The lexicographers explain it as **"as though you would abide for ever"**.

**What is blamed, then, is not the structure itself but the meaning loaded onto it.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the shared structure of the two verses:** in 128 *taʿbathūn* (purposelessness), in 129 *takhludūn* (the claim to permanence). **The two verses stand like opposites of one another — one to pursue no purpose at all, the other to pursue the greatest purpose.** And the two are side by side in the same list. **What they have in common is that both stand in the wrong place:** a structure is built neither to pass the time nor for everlastingness.

### وَإِذَا بَطَشْتُم بَطَشْتُمْ جَبَّارِين — the third offence

**The root ب-ط-ش** was analysed in two places across the series, and I rely on those treatments.

**`085-buruc.md` 85/12** (*inna baṭsha rabbika la-shadīd*) — the detailed analysis of the root was made there. **The description there: *baṭsh* is to grasp a thing violently and harshly, to seize by force.** And something important was recorded there: **the Qurʾān uses this root both of God and of wrongdoers.** Indeed this verse (al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/130) was already cited in `085-buruc.md` — *"This sentence Hūd says to his own people."*

**`054-kamer.md` 54/36** (*wa laqad andharahum baṭshatanā*) — there the difference between ***baṭsha*** (the *faʿla* pattern, a single occurrence) and *baṭsh* (the verbal noun) was tabulated. **I do not repeat it.**

**What is to be added here is the word order of the verse itself, and it is peculiar to this sūra.**

**The sentence has this structure: condition + apodosis, and both are the same verb.**

*Wa idhā **baṭashtum** — **baṭashtum** jabbārīn*

| Element | Expression | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| Condition | *Idhā baṭashtum* | **"When you seize"** |
| Apodosis | *Baṭashtum* | **"You seize"** |
| Circumstantial | *Jabbārīn* | **"As tyrants"** |

**The sentence thus stands like a tautology: "when you seize, you seize."** And all the weight is in the last word: ***jabbārīn***.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the structure itself:** the verse takes as its subject not the act but **the manner of the act**. Seizing in itself is not counted an offence — governing, punishing, defending may require seizing. **What is an offence is that the seizing is done *as tyrants*.**

**And the conditional particle is *idhā*, not *in*.** As was recorded in the thirty-first verse, **in Arabic *in* is used for the doubtful, *idhā* for the certain and recurring case.** The verse therefore does not say "if you seize"; **it says "whenever you seize"**. This announces that the behaviour is not an exception but **a rule**.

**جَبَّارِين** — root **ج-ب-ر**: the core the lexicographers give is **to set a broken bone back in place by force, to mend** (*jabīra* — a splint for a fracture; the *jabr* of mathematics — English *algebra* — comes from here as well). **And from there it passes to "to make someone do a thing by force, to make submit".**

**The *faʿʿāl* pattern announces an intensive: one who compels continually and much.**

**And the word is used in two directions in the Qurʾān, and this must be recorded:**

| Use | Example | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| **Of God** | *Al-ʿAzīzu'l-**Jabbār**u'l-Mutakabbir* (al-Ḥashr 59/23) | **The one who sets right, who carries His will through** |
| **Of a human** | **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/130**; *jabbāran shaqiyyā* (Maryam 19/32) | **A tyrant** |

**This distinction was treated in `059-hasr.md` and I do not repeat it.** The note there: the same attribute means "the one who sets right" of the one whose power is unlimited, and "a tyrant" of the one whose power is limited. **The difference lies in whom the attribute is found.**

**And the verse recorded at `050-kaf.md` 50/45 attaches here:** *wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-**jabbār*** — "you are not a tyrant over them". **The Qurʾān, that is, counts the attribute it forbids to the messenger as the deed of the people of ʿĀd.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### What this says to the present

The picture the three verses build together is concrete: **a society is measured by the structures it produces and by the force it uses.** And the measure is not what is done but **why and how it is done** — a monument with no purpose, a structure that hopes for everlastingness, a force used without measure.

**None of the verses forbids building, wealth or power.** What is criticised in all three is the same false assumption: **being permanent.** And one account later the sūra will show the same assumption once more from the mouth of Thamūd — *wa tanḥitūna mina'l-jibāli buyūtan fārihīn* (149).

---

## 26/131-135 — فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱلَّذِىٓ أَمَدَّكُم بِمَا تَعْلَمُونَ · أَمَدَّكُم بِأَنْعَٰمٍ وَبَنِينَ · وَجَنَّٰتٍ وَعُيُونٍ · إِنِّىٓ أَخَافُ عَلَيْكُمْ عَذَابَ يَوْمٍ عَظِيمٍ

*Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa'ttaqu'lladhī amaddakum bimā taʿlamūn · Amaddakum bi-anʿāmin wa banīn · Wa jannātin wa ʿuyūn · Innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm*

"**So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · Guard yourselves against Him who has supplied you with what you know: · He has supplied you with cattle and sons, · and gardens and springs. · Truly I fear for you the punishment of a great day.**"

### فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ — the second occurrence

**The sentence occurs for the second time in this account (126 and 131), and between them lies a three-verse list of offences.** What this means was treated in Table 4 of the section "The backbone of the sūra"; **I do not repeat it.**

### أَمَدَّكُم — root م-د-د

The root **م-د-د**: **to extend, to draw out, to keep going without break.** *Madd* — extension. Form IV (*amadda*) generally has the sense of **nourishing with help and support**: to supply continually.

**And the repetition of the verb must be recorded: twice in two verses.**

| Verse | Expression | Object |
|---|---|---|
| **132** | *Amaddakum **bimā taʿlamūn*** | **Indefinite** — "with what you know" |
| **133** | *Amaddakum **bi-anʿāmin wa banīn*** | **Definite** — it is enumerated |

**The verse says the general first, then enumerates.** In Arabic this is called **the general (*ijmāl*) followed by the detail (*tafṣīl*)**, and it serves to draw attention.

**And *bimā taʿlamūn* must be recorded: "with what you know".** No new knowledge is brought as proof; **what those addressed already know is made the proof.**

### Four favours — and their counterparts within the sūra

| Favour | Verse | Where else in the sūra |
|---|---|---|
| *Anʿām* — cattle | 133 | — |
| *Banīn* — sons | 133 | **88** — *lā yanfaʿu mālun wa lā **banūn*** |
| *Jannāt* — gardens | 134 | **57** — *min **jannātin** wa ʿuyūn* (taken from the people of Pharaoh) |
| *ʿUyūn* — springs | 134 | **57**, **147** (given to Thamūd) |

**The sūra thus uses the same two words (*jannāt* and *ʿuyūn*) of three different peoples:** they are taken from the people of Pharaoh (57), given to ʿĀd (134), given to Thamūd (147).

**This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.** And **as my own reading** I record this: the sūra gives the same thing to three different peoples in the same words, and in all three the outcome is the same. **The kind of the favour does not change; the attitude of those who receive it changes** — and the seal follows all three with the same sentence.

### إِنِّىٓ أَخَافُ عَلَيْكُمْ — the direction of the fear

**And the word order of the sentence must be recorded: *akhāfu ʿalaykum* — "I fear for you".**

**The preposition is *ʿalā*.** Not *akhāfu minkum* ("I fear you") but *akhāfu ʿalaykum* ("I fear on your behalf").

**And this can be compared with the fourteenth verse of the sūra:** Moses had said *fa-**akhāfu** an yaqtulūn* — fear for himself. **Here Hūd fears for those he addresses.**

| Verse | Who | Expression | Direction of the fear |
|---|---|---|---|
| **12** | Moses | *Akhāfu an yukadhdhibūn* | **For himself** |
| **14** | Moses | *Fa-akhāfu an yaqtulūn* | **For himself** |
| **135** | **Hūd** | *Akhāfu **ʿalaykum*** | **For those he addresses** |

**This is a difference that can be verified from the text.** And it stands on the same line as the third verse of the sūra: **the burden the messenger carries for those he addresses.**

**عَذَابَ يَوْمٍ عَظِيم** — the construction occurs three times in the sūra: **135** (Hūd), **156** (Ṣāliḥ), **189** (Shuʿayb — as *ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm*). **The same expression in three different accounts.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

---

## 26/136-140 — قَالُوا۟ سَوَآءٌ عَلَيْنَآ أَوَعَظْتَ أَمْ لَمْ تَكُن مِّنَ ٱلْوَٰعِظِينَ · إِنْ هَٰذَآ إِلَّا خُلُقُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ · وَمَا نَحْنُ بِمُعَذَّبِينَ · فَكَذَّبُوهُ فَأَهْلَكْنَٰهُمْ ۗ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً ۖ وَمَا كَانَ أَكْثَرُهُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ · وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

*Qālū sawāʾun ʿalaynā a-waʿaẓta am lam takun mina'l-wāʿiẓīn · In hādhā illā khuluqu'l-awwalīn · Wa mā naḥnu bi-muʿadhdhabīn · Fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-ahlaknāhum; inna fī dhālika la-āyatan wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn · Wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*

"They said: '**It is all one to us whether you admonish or are not of those who admonish. · This is nothing but the custom of the ancients. · And we are not going to be punished.**' · **So they denied him, and We destroyed them.** In this there is surely a sign; but most of them were not believers. · And your Lord — He indeed — is the Mighty, the Merciful."

### سَوَآءٌ عَلَيْنَا — the expression of indifference

**The root س-و-ي** was analysed in the ninety-eighth verse (*nusawwīkum*), and the sense "to hold equal" was recorded there. **Here the same root comes as *sawāʾ*: "equal, it makes no difference".**

**And a point of word order:** the answer does not discuss the content of the admonition. **It equates the admonition's existing with its not existing.**

**The same mould occurs in several places in the Qurʾān:** *sawāʾun ʿalayhim a-andhartahum am lam tundhirhum lā yuʾminūn* (al-Baqara 2/6; Yā Sīn 36/10 — treated in `036-yasin.md`). **The difference must be recorded: there the narrator frames the sentence; here the people themselves say it.**

| Place | Who says it |
|---|---|
| **Al-Baqara 2/6, Yā Sīn 36/10** | **The narrator** — as a judgement |
| **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/136** | **The people themselves** — as a confession |

**This is a difference that can be verified from the text.**

**And the mould *a-waʿaẓta am lam takun mina'l-wāʿiẓīn* must be recorded:** the two sides are not symmetrical. The first side is a verb (*waʿaẓta* — you admonished), the second side **a noun** (*lam takun mina'l-wāʿiẓīn* — you were not of those who admonish).

**The transmitted explanation: the second side negates not the act but the attribute — that is, not "not to admonish" but "not to be an admonisher".** This is another instance of the "assignment to a group" mould that occurs often in the sūra (tabulated in the section on 26/29-31).

### خُلُقُ ٱلْأَوَّلِين — two readings of the word

**And the verse is one of the places where a variant reading changes the sense.**

| Reading | Word | Root | Sense |
|---|---|---|---|
| ***khuluq*** | خُلُق | **خ-ل-ق** (trait, character) | **"The custom, the way of the ancients"** — this is something the ancients made up |
| ***khalq*** | خَلْق | **خ-ل-ق** (creating) | **"The fabrication of the ancients"** or **"what has come down from of old"** |

**Both readings are transmitted, and both are from the same root; the difference is in the vowelling.** The difference of sense is not great: **on either reading the people count the admonition as "something that has been going on from of old".**

**And there are two possible directions, and both are transmitted:**

| Direction | Its meaning |
|---|---|
| **It looks to the admonition** | "This threat is a tale the ancients made up" |
| **It looks to their own life** | "What we do is what our forefathers did too" |

**I do not impose a preference.** The second direction belongs to the same family as the proof from the forefathers in the seventy-fourth verse — **it was treated in `043-zuhruf.md` and `031-lokman.md` and I rely on those treatments.**

### وَمَا نَحْنُ بِمُعَذَّبِين — the third sentence

**And the people's answer is completed in three steps:**

| Order | Sentence | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Sawāʾun ʿalaynā* | **Declaring the admonition ineffective** |
| 2 | *In hādhā illā khuluqu'l-awwalīn* | **Denying the source of the admonition** |
| 3 | *Wa mā naḥnu bi-muʿadhdhabīn* | **Denying the outcome** |

**The third sentence is reinforced with the *mā … bi-* mould** (this mould was treated in the hundred and fourteenth verse): **"we are never going to be punished."**

**And the next verse answers this in a single sentence: *fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-**ahlaknāhum***.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the people say *mā naḥnu bi-muʿadhdhabīn*; the text says *ahlaknāhum*. **There is not even one verse between the two sentences.** And how the destruction came about is not recounted — only that it did.

### The addition at the head of the seal — 139

**The hundred and thirty-ninth verse is one of the two of the sūra's eight seals that carry an addition** (the other is 158). **This was treated in Table 1 of the section "The backbone of the sūra", and an explanation was proposed there; I do not repeat it.**

**What is to be added here is a note on word order: the addition consists of two verbs, and both are joined with *fāʾ*.**

*Fa-**kadhdhabūhu** fa-**ahlaknāhum***

**فَ** (*fāʾ al-taʿqīb*) announces succession without interval. **No distance, that is, is put between the two verbs: they denied, We destroyed.**

**And as was recorded in `037-saffat.md`, this is precisely the difference between *fāʾ* and *thumma*:** *thumma* puts time in between, *fāʾ* does not. **In the account of Noah the destruction had come with *thumma*** (*thumma aghraqnā*, 120) — **there was time enough there for an ark to be built.** Here there is *fāʾ*.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two particles.** This is an explanation, not a ruling.

### The sealing of the account — 139-140

**The fifth seal of the sūra. The mould was treated at 26/8-9.**

---

## 26/141-145 — كَذَّبَتْ ثَمُودُ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · إِذْ قَالَ لَهُمْ أَخُوهُمْ صَٰلِحٌ أَلَا تَتَّقُونَ · إِنِّى لَكُمْ رَسُولٌ أَمِينٌ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَمَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَجْرٍ ۖ إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Kadhdhabat Thamūdu'l-mursalīn · Idh qāla lahum akhūhum Ṣāliḥun alā tattaqūn · Innī lakum rasūlun amīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin; in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*

"**Thamūd too denied the messengers.** · When their brother Ṣāliḥ said to them: '**Will you not guard yourselves? · I am a trustworthy messenger sent to you. · So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · I ask you no wage for it; my wage falls only upon the Lord of all the worlds.**'"

### The shared frame — tabulated above

**All five of the five verses are the shared mould of the sūra; the word analyses were made in the introductory section on 26/105-191 and are not repeated.**

### ثَمُود and the place of the people of Ṣāliḥ

**Thamūd is one of the peoples the Qurʾān names most often, and it has occurred in many places in this commentary:** `054-kamer.md` 54/23-31 (the she-camel and the *ṣayḥa*), `089-fecr.md` 89/9 (*alladhīna jābū'ṣ-ṣakhra bi'l-wād* — "those who hollowed the rocks in the valley"), `091-sems.md` 91/11-15 (those who hamstrung the she-camel), `069-hakka.md` 69/5 (*fa-uhlikū bi'ṭ-ṭāghiya*), `051-zariyat.md` 51/43-45.

**Al-Shuʿarāʾ's own contribution is the enumeration of *what the people stand to lose*** (146-149). **The other sūras recount the she-camel and the destruction; al-Shuʿarāʾ first counts what they possess.**

---

## 26/146-152 — أَتُتْرَكُونَ فِى مَا هَٰهُنَآ ءَامِنِينَ · فِى جَنَّٰتٍ وَعُيُونٍ · وَزُرُوعٍ وَنَخْلٍ طَلْعُهَا هَضِيمٌ · وَتَنْحِتُونَ مِنَ ٱلْجِبَالِ بُيُوتًا فَٰرِهِينَ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَلَا تُطِيعُوٓا۟ أَمْرَ ٱلْمُسْرِفِينَ · ٱلَّذِينَ يُفْسِدُونَ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَلَا يُصْلِحُونَ

*A-tutrakūna fī mā hāhunā āminīn · Fī jannātin wa ʿuyūn · Wa zurūʿin wa nakhlin ṭalʿuhā haḍīm · Wa tanḥitūna mina'l-jibāli buyūtan fārihīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa lā tuṭīʿū amra'l-musrifīn · Alladhīna yufsidūna fi'l-arḍi wa lā yuṣliḥūn*

"**Will you be left secure in what is here — · in gardens and springs, · in fields and palm trees with their laden spathes? · And you carve houses out of the mountains with skill.** · **So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · And do not obey the command of those who exceed all measure — · who spread corruption in the earth and do not set it right.**"

### أَتُتْرَكُونَ — the mould of the question

The root **ت-ر-ك**: **to leave, to abandon, to leave as it is.**

**And the verb is passive: *tutrakūna* — "will you be left?"** By whom they will be left is not said. **The question, that is, looks not to the act of an agent but to a state.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the question opens with *a-* and is a question of denial (*istifhām inkārī*)** — it expects no answer; it seeks to draw a "no".

**فِى مَا هَٰهُنَآ** — word for word: "**in the midst of what is here.**" **هَٰهُنَا** — "here" (a demonstrative of near place).

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the vagueness of the expression:** the verse does not say "in your possessions" or "in your land". **It says "in the midst of what is here"** — and then enumerates. **The question first names a general feeling of security, then sets out the things that feeling rests on.**

**ءَامِنِين** — root **أ-م-ن** (analysed above for *amīn*): **to be safe.**

**And an overlap within the sūra must be recorded:** the same root is the attribute by which Ṣāliḥ introduces himself — *innī lakum rasūlun **amīn*** (143). **Three verses lie between the two.**

| Verse | Word | Of whom / of what |
|---|---|---|
| **143** | *amīn* | **The messenger** — trustworthy |
| **146** | *āminīn* | **The people** — secure |

**The same root, two different directions: one who gives security, and those who suppose themselves secure.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text; what it means is interpretation.

### Four favours — the enumeration

| Order | Word | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Jannāt* | **Gardens** |
| 2 | *ʿUyūn* | **Springs** |
| 3 | *Zurūʿ* | **Fields** |
| 4 | *Nakhl* | **Palm trees** |

**The first two are the same as those given to the people of ʿĀd (134).** Two more are added for Thamūd: **crops and dates** — that is, **agriculture.**

**طَلْعُهَا هَضِيم** — a particular description for the spathe of the palm.

**طَلْع** — the first bud the palm tree puts out, the cluster while still in its sheath. The word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān (al-Anʿām 6/99; Qāf 50/10 — treated in `050-kaf.md`).

**هَضِيم** — root **ه-ض-م**: **to soften, to be crushed, to digest** (*haḍm* is from the same root). The renderings transmitted for *haḍīm*:

| Rendering | Ground |
|---|---|
| **Soft, tender** | The root's sense of "softening" |
| **Layered, dense, full** | A cluster grown into itself from the abundance of fruit |
| **Drooping with its weight** | A cluster bending because it is full |
