**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** What the three have in common: **fruitfulness and ripeness.**

**I record this as an observation:** the verse names the palm not as a tree but **by the state of its fruit**. **What is counted, that is, is not property but what the property yields.**

### وَتَنْحِتُونَ مِنَ ٱلْجِبَالِ بُيُوتًا فَٰرِهِين — carving houses out of mountains

The root **ن-ح-ت**: **to carve, to shape by cutting.** It is used of stone and of wood.

**And the verb must be recorded: the structure is not *built*, it is *carved*.** These are two different works: to build is to set stone upon stone, to carve is to make **by taking away from existing rock**.

**And the verse recorded at `089-fecr.md` 89/9 attaches here:** *wa Thamūda'lladhīna **jābū'ṣ-ṣakhra** bi'l-wād* — "and Thamūd, **who hollowed the rocks** in the valley". **The same people, the same work, two different verbs (*naḥata* and *jāba*).** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**فَٰرِهِين** — root **ف-ر-ه**, and the word has been read in two different directions:

| Reading | Sense | The sentence's meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **With skill, skilfully** | *Fārih* — expert, skilled | "You carve houses with skill" — **it describes the skill** |
| **Exultantly, boastfully** | *Fārih* — grown insolent from ease | "You carve houses exultantly" — **it describes the attitude** |

**Both readings are transmitted, and the root of the word admits of both.** Among the variant readings the reading *fariḥīn* is also transmitted, and it brings forward the sense of "joy, insolence". **I do not impose a preference.**

**And I record an observation:** both senses do work in the same list. **If the sense of skill is taken, the verse takes as its subject not the craftsmanship but what the craftsmanship is spent on** — because immediately after comes "guard yourselves against God". **If the sense of insolence is taken, the verse takes the attitude directly as its subject.**

### ʿĀd and Thamūd — two lists of building

**And one of the clearest internal overlaps of the sūra is here:**

| | **ʿĀd — 128-129** | **Thamūd — 149** |
|---|---|---|
| **Verb** | *Tabnūna* (building), *tattakhidhūna* (taking) | *Tanḥitūna* (carving) |
| **Object** | *Āyatan* (a marker), *maṣāniʿa* (strongholds) | *Buyūtan* (houses) |
| **Quality** | *Taʿbathūn* (to no purpose), *takhludūn* (abiding for ever) | *Fārihīn* (with skill / exultantly) |
| **Material** | What is made | **The mountain** |

**Both peoples are named by their buildings, and in both what is criticised is not the building itself but the meaning loaded onto it.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And as was recorded in `085-buruc.md` and `089-fecr.md`, the Qurʾān often names these two peoples side by side.** Al-Shuʿarāʾ keeps the same order: **ʿĀd (123-140), then Thamūd (141-159).**

### وَلَا تُطِيعُوٓا۟ أَمْرَ ٱلْمُسْرِفِين — the second command

**And here a prohibition is set opposite the command *wa aṭīʿūn* ("obey me") repeated in the five accounts: *wa lā tuṭīʿū amra'l-musrifīn*.**

**The same root (ط-و-ع), two verses apart, one a command and one a prohibition.**

| Verse | Expression | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **150** | *Wa **aṭīʿūn*** | **The messenger** — a command |
| **151** | *Wa lā **tuṭīʿū** amra'l-musrifīn* | **Those who exceed all measure** — a prohibition |

**This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and among the five accounts it is present only with Ṣāliḥ.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the adjacency of the two verses:** the call does not only say "obey me"; **it also says whose command is not to be obeyed.** Obedience does not stand in a void — one direction is chosen, the other left.

The root **س-ر-ف**: **to overstep the bound, to lose the measure.** *Isrāf* means passing the limit not only in spending but **in every field**. **The root was treated in `040-gafir.md` and at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/5** (*an kuntum qawman musrifīn*).

**And the word *amr* must be recorded:** the prohibition is set not on the persons but on **their command**: *lā tuṭīʿū **amra**'l-musrifīn.* **The matter, that is, is not the identity of the persons but the instruction they give.**

### ٱلَّذِينَ يُفْسِدُونَ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَلَا يُصْلِحُون — the description itself

**And the question of who the *musrifīn* are is answered in the next verse by a description. This accords with the principle of the sūra (recorded in the section on 26/10-11): the judgement is built not on identity but on attribute.**

The root **ف-س-د**: **the breakdown of a thing's order, its becoming useless.** The root **ص-ل-ح**: **to become sound, to become fit and useful.** The two roots are constantly set against one another in the Qurʾān.

**And a point of word order must be recorded: two verbs, one positive and one negative.**

*Yufsidūna fi'l-arḍi **wa lā yuṣliḥūn***

**The transmitted explanation:** the second clause is not a repetition of the first. **The first clause says what is done, the second what is not done.** The description, that is, has two wings: **to corrupt and not to set right.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the second clause has been added:** *yufsidūn* alone would have sufficed. **The added clause sets down, beside corrupting, a separate note of failing to set right.** A corrupter's defending himself with "I am not doing anything" is met by the second wing of the verse.

---

## 26/153-159 — قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّمَآ أَنتَ مِنَ ٱلْمُسَحَّرِينَ · مَآ أَنتَ إِلَّا بَشَرٌ مِّثْلُنَا فَأْتِ بِـَٔايَةٍ إِن كُنتَ مِنَ ٱلصَّٰدِقِينَ · قَالَ هَٰذِهِۦ نَاقَةٌ لَّهَا شِرْبٌ وَلَكُمْ شِرْبُ يَوْمٍ مَّعْلُومٍ · وَلَا تَمَسُّوهَا بِسُوٓءٍ فَيَأْخُذَكُمْ عَذَابُ يَوْمٍ عَظِيمٍ · فَعَقَرُوهَا فَأَصْبَحُوا۟ نَٰدِمِينَ · فَأَخَذَهُمُ ٱلْعَذَابُ ۗ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً ۖ وَمَا كَانَ أَكْثَرُهُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ · وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

*Qālū innamā anta mina'l-musaḥḥarīn · Mā anta illā basharun mithlunā fa'ti bi-āyatin in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn · Qāla hādhihi nāqatun lahā shirbun wa lakum shirbu yawmin maʿlūm · Wa lā tamassūhā bi-sūʾin fa-yaʾkhudhakum ʿadhābu yawmin ʿaẓīm · Fa-ʿaqarūhā fa-aṣbaḥū nādimīn · Fa-akhadhahumu'l-ʿadhāb; inna fī dhālika la-āyatan wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn · Wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*

"They said: '**You are only one of the bewitched. · You are nothing but a mortal like us. Bring a sign, if you are one of the truthful!**' · He said: '**This is a she-camel. She has a turn to drink, and you have a turn on a known day. · Do not touch her with harm, or the punishment of a great day will seize you.**' · **But they hamstrung her; then they became regretful. · And the punishment seized them.** In this there is surely a sign; but most of them were not believers. · And your Lord — He indeed — is the Mighty, the Merciful."

### ٱلْمُسَحَّرِين — two readings of the word

**The sentence occurs twice in the sūra and is exactly the same in the mouths of two peoples:**

| Verse | Who says it | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **153** | **Thamūd** | To Ṣāliḥ |
| **185** | **The people of al-Ayka** | To Shuʿayb |

**This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.**

**مُسَحَّر** — root **س-ح-ر**, the passive participle of Form II. **And the word has been read in two different directions:**

| Reading | The sense taken of the root | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Bewitched** | *Siḥr* — sorcery | "Sorcery has been worked on you, your mind is confused" |
| **One who has a lung, who eats and drinks** | *Saḥar* — the lung | "You too are a human being who eats and drinks like us" |

**The second reading is transmitted among the lexicographers: *saḥar* in Arabic means the lung, and *musaḥḥar* means "possessor of a lung", that is, a mortal.**

**And the next verse supports the second reading:** *mā anta illā **basharun mithlunā*** — "you are nothing but a mortal like us". **Verse 154, that is, stands as though it were explaining verse 153.**

**I do not impose a preference; but I record this contextual link as a datum of the text.**

**And what the two readings have in common is this:** both **classify the messenger.** Either "a man whose mind is disordered" or "an ordinary human being". **What he says is not discussed.**

**This is a technique repeated in the sūra and deserves to be tabulated:**

| Verse | Accusation | Of whom | Who says it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **27** | *La-majnūn* | **Moses** | Pharaoh |
| **34** | *Sāḥirun ʿalīm* | **Moses** | Pharaoh |
| **153** | *Mina'l-musaḥḥarīn* | **Ṣāliḥ** | Thamūd |
| **185** | *Mina'l-musaḥḥarīn* | **Shuʿayb** | The people of al-Ayka |
| **186** | *La-mina'l-kādhibīn* | **Shuʿayb** | The people of al-Ayka |
| **224-226** | *Poet* (indirectly) | **The messenger addressed** | (the accusation the sūra answers) |

**All the accusations directed at the five messengers in the sūra are thus of the same kind: the speech is not discussed, the speaker is classified.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text **and it explains why the final section of the sūra takes poets as its subject.** This link will be treated in the section on 224-227.

### فَأْتِ بِـَٔايَةٍ — the demand for a miracle

**And this demand meets the fourth verse of the sūra.**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **4** | *In nashaʾ nunazzil ʿalayhim mina's-samāʾi **āyatan*** | **God** — it is not sent down |
| **31** | *Fa'ti bihi in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn* | **Pharaoh** — a demand |
| **154** | *Fa'ti bi-**āyatin** in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn* | **Thamūd** — a demand |
| **187** | *Fa-asqiṭ ʿalaynā kisafan mina's-samāʾ* | **The people of al-Ayka** — a demand |

**Three peoples, the same demand three times.** And 31 and 154 are almost exactly the same (*in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn*).

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the comparison of these four verses:** in the fourth verse the sūra had said "if it were willed a sign would be sent down from heaven and the necks would bow". **It then shows three peoples asking for the same thing.** And to Thamūd **a sign is given** (155) — and the outcome does not change. **The sūra, that is, tests the judgement of the fourth verse by an account.**

### نَاقَة — the she-camel

The root **ن-و-ق**: a female camel. **The Qurʾān speaks of this she-camel in many places** (al-Aʿrāf 7/73; Hūd 11/64; al-Isrāʾ 17/59; al-Qamar 54/27; al-Shams 91/13) **and it was treated in `054-kamer.md` and `091-sems.md`.**

**What is peculiar to al-Shuʿarāʾ's telling must be recorded: here it is not said how the she-camel came.** *Hādhihi nāqatun* — "this is a she-camel". **It is only pointed to.**

**I do not enter into a detail the Qurʾān does not give:** reports are transmitted that the she-camel came out of a rock; this is not in the Qurʾān.

### شِرْب — the turn

The root **ش-ر-ب**: to drink. *Shirb* — **a share of drinking water, a turn.**

**And the order itself must be recorded: the water is apportioned day by day.** One day for the she-camel, one for the people. **And at `054-kamer.md` 54/28 the same order is stated more plainly:** *wa nabbiʾhum anna'l-māʾa qismatun baynahum* — "and inform them that the water is apportioned among them".

**I record this as an observation:** the subject of the trial is not a belief but **keeping to an order of sharing**. The one thing asked of the people is not to break the turn of the water.

### وَلَا تَمَسُّوهَا بِسُوٓءٍ — the word order of the prohibition

**The prohibition is not "do not kill her":** *lā tamassūhā bi-sūʾin* — "**do not touch her with harm**".

The root **م-س-س**: **to touch, to come into contact.** The verb is used of the lightest contact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the verb:** the prohibition begins not from the extreme act (killing) but **from the smallest contact**. **And the people break the prohibition at the extreme point: *fa-ʿaqarūhā*.**

The root **ع-ق-ر**: the sense the lexicographers give is **to cut the leg tendon of an animal, to wound it at the root.** The word is not "to kill" (*qatala*); **it is to bring the camel down by cutting its leg**, and this was the Arabs' method of slaughtering a camel.

### فَأَصْبَحُوا۟ نَٰدِمِين — the place of the regret

**And the verse contains the most striking sentence of the account: the people become regretful.**

The root **ن-د-م**: **to feel inward regret for a deed done.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the regret comes *before* the punishment.**

| Verse | Event |
|---|---|
| **157** | *Fa-ʿaqarūhā* → *fa-aṣbaḥū **nādimīn*** |
| **158** | *Fa-akhadhahumu'l-ʿadhāb* |

**The order, then, is this: the deed → regret → the punishment.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the two verses:** the regret does not stop the punishment. **And the verse does not say what kind of regret it was** — repentance, or the rueful regret felt on seeing the consequence? **The text does not distinguish.**

**The transmitted explanations:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| **They became regretful on seeing the signs of the punishment** | Elsewhere the Qurʾān speaks of a respite of three days (Hūd 11/65) |
| **They became regretful on grasping the consequence of what they had done** | No sign is named in the verse |

**I do not impose a preference.** What is to be recorded is this: **the Qurʾān records elsewhere too that not every kind of regret yields the same outcome** — *fa-lam yaku yanfaʿuhum īmānuhum lammā raʾaw baʾsanā* (al-Muʾmin 40/85 — treated in `040-gafir.md`).

### The addition at the head of the seal — 158

**The hundred and fifty-eighth verse is the second of the sūra's eight seals to carry an addition** (the other is 139). **This was treated in Table 1 of the section "The backbone of the sūra"; I do not repeat it.**

**What is to be added here is a note on a word:** the addition is in the form *fa-**akhadhahum**u'l-ʿadhāb*, and the verb is the same as the verb Ṣāliḥ used in the hundred and fifty-sixth verse: *fa-**yaʾkhudhakum** ʿadhābu yawmin ʿaẓīm.*

**The verb used in the warning, that is, is repeated unchanged in the fulfilment.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text. **And it stands on the same line as the principle recorded in `085-buruc.md`: the outcome is announced in the language of the warning.**

---

## 26/160-166 — كَذَّبَتْ قَوْمُ لُوطٍ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · إِذْ قَالَ لَهُمْ أَخُوهُمْ لُوطٌ أَلَا تَتَّقُونَ · إِنِّى لَكُمْ رَسُولٌ أَمِينٌ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَمَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَجْرٍ ۖ إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ · أَتَأْتُونَ ٱلذُّكْرَانَ مِنَ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ · وَتَذَرُونَ مَا خَلَقَ لَكُمْ رَبُّكُم مِّنْ أَزْوَٰجِكُم ۚ بَلْ أَنتُمْ قَوْمٌ عَادُونَ

*Kadhdhabat qawmu Lūṭin'il-mursalīn · Idh qāla lahum akhūhum Lūṭun alā tattaqūn · Innī lakum rasūlun amīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin; in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · A-taʾtūna'dh-dhukrāna mina'l-ʿālamīn · Wa tadharūna mā khalaqa lakum rabbukum min azwājikum; bal antum qawmun ʿādūn*

"**The people of Lot too denied the messengers.** · When their brother Lot said to them: '**Will you not guard yourselves? · I am a trustworthy messenger sent to you. · So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · I ask you no wage for it; my wage falls only upon the Lord of all the worlds. · Do you go to the males out of all the worlds · and leave the mates your Lord created for you? Indeed you are a people who overstep the bound.**'"

### The shared frame — tabulated above

**The first five verses are the shared mould of the sūra; the word analyses were made in the introductory section on 26/105-191 and are not repeated.**

**And in this account the sentence *fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn* occurs once (163), with no second occurrence.** What this means was treated in Table 4 of the section "The backbone of the sūra".

### The accusation in two verses

**And in the account of Lot the offence is recounted in two verses and with two verbs:**

| Verse | Verb | What |
|---|---|---|
| **165** | *Taʾtūna* — you go | **What is done** |
| **166** | *Tadharūna* — you leave | **What is left** |

**The accusation, that is, is not single-winged: the doing of one thing and the leaving of another are named together.**

**This is the brother of the *yufsidūna … wa lā yuṣliḥūn* structure in the account of Ṣāliḥ (152).** In both accounts the description **has two wings**. This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### ٱلذُّكْرَانَ مِنَ ٱلْعَٰلَمِين — word order

**ذُكْرَان** — one of the plurals of *dhakar*: **males.**

**And *mina'l-ʿālamīn* must be recorded: "out of all the worlds".** The explanation the lexicographers give: the expression widens the **scope** of the act — whether from their own people or not.

**And the word uses the same word as the construction *rabbu'l-ʿālamīn*, which occurs eleven times in the sūra.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text: the word *ʿālamīn* occurs in the sūra both as the whole to which the Lord is attributed and, here, as the whole out of which the act makes its selection.

### وَتَذَرُونَ مَا خَلَقَ لَكُمْ رَبُّكُم — the second wing

The root **و-ذ-ر**: **to abandon, to leave.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the sentence has three elements, and all three look to the same side:**

| Element | Expression | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Mā **khalaqa*** | **What was created** |
| 2 | ***Lakum*** | **For you** |
| 3 | ***Rabbukum*** | **By your Lord** |

**What is abandoned is thus qualified three times:** created, for you, by your Lord. **The sentence stresses not what is left but whom it comes from.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure.**

**And the word *azwājikum* must be recorded.** The root **ز-و-ج** was analysed in the seventh verse of the sūra (*min kulli zawjin karīm*), and the description recorded there holds here too: ***zawj* is not "a pair" but "one member of a pair"** — anything that has a mate.

**The sūra thus uses the same root in two places: once of plants (7) and once of human beings (166).** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

### عَادُون — those who overstep the bound

The root **ع-د-و**: **to overstep the limit, to pass the bound, to transgress.** From the same root: *ʿaduww* (enemy — it occurred in the seventy-seventh verse) and *ʿudwān* (aggression).

**And the particle *bal* must be recorded:** *bal antum qawmun ʿādūn.* **In the seventy-fourth verse the same particle was in the people's mouth** (*bal wajadnā ābāʾanā*); here it is in the messenger's. **The particle announces the leaving of one statement and the passing to the real point:** "the real matter is that you are a people who overstep the bound."

**And that the description rests on an attribute must be recorded:** *qawmun ʿādūn* is not the name of a community but **an attribute**. This is the same as the principle recorded in the eleventh verse of the sūra and accords with the principle recorded in `STYLE.md`: **the judgement is built not on who they are but on what they do.**

---

## 26/167-175 — قَالُوا۟ لَئِن لَّمْ تَنتَهِ يَٰلُوطُ لَتَكُونَنَّ مِنَ ٱلْمُخْرَجِينَ · قَالَ إِنِّى لِعَمَلِكُم مِّنَ ٱلْقَالِينَ · رَبِّ نَجِّنِى وَأَهْلِى مِمَّا يَعْمَلُونَ · فَنَجَّيْنَٰهُ وَأَهْلَهُۥٓ أَجْمَعِينَ · إِلَّا عَجُوزًا فِى ٱلْغَٰبِرِينَ · ثُمَّ دَمَّرْنَا ٱلْءَاخَرِينَ · وَأَمْطَرْنَا عَلَيْهِم مَّطَرًا ۖ فَسَآءَ مَطَرُ ٱلْمُنذَرِينَ · إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً ۖ وَمَا كَانَ أَكْثَرُهُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ · وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

*Qālū la-in lam tantahi yā Lūṭu la-takūnanna mina'l-mukhrajīn · Qāla innī li-ʿamalikum mina'l-qālīn · Rabbi najjinī wa ahlī mimmā yaʿmalūn · Fa-najjaynāhu wa ahlahu ajmaʿīn · Illā ʿajūzan fi'l-ghābirīn · Thumma dammarna'l-ākharīn · Wa amṭarnā ʿalayhim maṭarā; fa-sāʾa maṭaru'l-mundharīn · Inna fī dhālika la-āyatan wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn · Wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*

"They said: '**Lot! If you do not stop, you will surely be among those driven out!**' · He said: '**I am one of those who detest what you do. · My Lord! Save me and my household from what they do.**' · **So We saved him and all his household — · save an old woman among those who stayed behind. · Then We razed the others · and rained upon them a rain. How evil is the rain of those who were warned!**' · In this there is surely a sign; but most of them were not believers. · And your Lord — He indeed — is the Mighty, the Merciful."

### The mould of the threat — the second occurrence

**The sentence has exactly the same structure as the hundred and sixteenth verse, with one word of difference.** This was tabulated in the section on 26/116-122; **I do not repeat it.**

**What is to be added here is a note on the difference:** Noah is threatened with **stoning** (*marjūmīn*), Lot with **expulsion** (*mukhrajīn*).

**And the word *mukhrajīn* is one link in the chain of verbs of driving out that occur in the sūra:**

| Verse | Expression | Who drives out / is driven out |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *Yurīdu an **yukhrijakum** min arḍikum* | Pharaoh's claim |
| **57** | *Fa-**akhrajnāhum** min jannātin wa ʿuyūn* | God — the people of Pharaoh |
| **167** | *La-takūnanna mina'l-**mukhrajīn*** | The people of Lot — a threat |

**The same root (خ-ر-ج), three different scenes.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text. **And in all three "driving from the land" is used as a display of power.**

### مِّنَ ٱلْقَالِين — a rare word

The root **ق-ل-ي**: **to dislike intensely, to loathe, to detest.** The lexicographers record that the word is **the exact opposite of love**, and note that another sense of the verb *qalā* is **to roast** (*maqlī* — roasted); **they make the link between the two senses with the picture of "burning inside".**

**In this pattern the word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** Another derivative of the root occurs at al-Ḍuḥā 93/3: *mā waddaʿaka rabbuka wa mā **qalā*** — "your Lord has neither forsaken you nor turned against you" (treated in `093-duha.md`).

**And a point of word order must be recorded, and it accords with the principle of the sūra:**

***Innī li-ʿamalikum mina'l-qālīn*** — "I am one of those who detest **what you do**."

**The object of the loathing is not the persons but the *deed*.** The verse does not say *innī lakum mina'l-qālīn* ("I am one of those who detest you").

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the preposition (*li-ʿamalikum*):** the distinction is plain in the sentence — the act is separated from the actor. **And this is the textual counterpart of the principle recorded in `STYLE.md`: the judgement is built not on the persons themselves but on the act described.**

**And the next verse continues the same distinction:** *najjinī wa ahlī **mimmā yaʿmalūn*** — "save me from **what they do**", not "from them".

**In both verses the same root (ع-م-ل) and the same distinction.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### إِلَّا عَجُوزًا فِى ٱلْغَٰبِرِين — the exception

**عَجُوز** — an old woman. **The Qurʾān gives no name here;** that it is Lot's wife is understood from other places in the Qurʾān (Hūd 11/81; al-Taḥrīm 66/10 — treated in `066-tahrim.md`).

The root **غ-ب-ر**: **to remain behind, to be left over.** *Ghābir* — one who stays behind, who remains after those who have gone. **The word is repeated several times in the Qurʾān for this event** (al-Aʿrāf 7/83; al-Ḥijr 15/60; al-Naml 27/57).

**And the principle recorded in `066-tahrim.md` attaches here:** there, with the wives of Noah and of Lot given as examples, it was treated that **closeness does not save**. **I rely on the note there.**

**And a point of word order:** the exception comes **immediately after** the sentence of deliverance and limits the word *ajmaʿīn* ("all"). **"All" is said, and then one person is taken out.**

### دَمَّرْنَا and أَمْطَرْنَا — two verbs

The root **د-م-ر**: **to destroy, to raze.** Form II (*dammara*) intensifies. **And at `037-saffat.md` 37/136 the same verb occurs of the same people:** *thumma **dammarna**'l-ākharīn* — **exactly the same sentence.**

**This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it binds the Lot sections of the two sūras together.** As was recorded in `037-saffat.md`, the Lot section there did not receive the closing seal; **in al-Shuʿarāʾ it does.**

The root **م-ط-ر**: rain. **And a point of word order must be recorded: there is a cognate accusative.**

*Wa amṭarnā ʿalayhim **maṭarā***

**The verb is reinforced with a verbal noun from its own root.** And in Arabic this construction **qualifies when it takes an adjective**; here the qualification comes in the following clause: *fa-sāʾa maṭaru'l-mundharīn* — "how evil is the rain of those who were warned."

**And what it was that rained is not said.** The Qurʾān specifies it elsewhere (*ḥijāratan min sijjīl* — Hūd 11/82; al-Ḥijr 15/74). **Al-Shuʿarāʾ does not specify.** This is a difference that can be verified from the text.

**ٱلْمُنذَرِين** — a passive participle: **those who have been warned.** **The sentence thus records with a word that the punishment did not come without warning.** And this will tie to the two hundred and eighth verse of the sūra: *wa mā ahlaknā min qaryatin illā lahā **mundhirūn***.

**This is an overlap that can be verified from the text:** here **those warned** (passive), there **those who warn** (active) — the same root.

### The sealing of the account — 174-175

**The seventh seal of the sūra. The mould was treated at 26/8-9.**

---

## 26/176-180 — كَذَّبَ أَصْحَٰبُ لْـَٔيْكَةِ ٱلْمُرْسَلِينَ · إِذْ قَالَ لَهُمْ شُعَيْبٌ أَلَا تَتَّقُونَ · إِنِّى لَكُمْ رَسُولٌ أَمِينٌ · فَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُونِ · وَمَآ أَسْـَٔلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ مِنْ أَجْرٍ ۖ إِنْ أَجْرِىَ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Kadhdhaba aṣḥābu'l-Aykati'l-mursalīn · Idh qāla lahum Shuʿaybun alā tattaqūn · Innī lakum rasūlun amīn · Fa'ttaqū'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Wa mā asʾalukum ʿalayhi min ajrin; in ajriya illā ʿalā rabbi'l-ʿālamīn*

"**The people of al-Ayka too denied the messengers.** · When Shuʿayb said to them: '**Will you not guard yourselves? · I am a trustworthy messenger sent to you. · So guard yourselves against God and obey me. · I ask you no wage for it; my wage falls only upon the Lord of all the worlds.**'"

### Two breaks in the mould

**These five verses are the fifth and last repetition of the sūra's shared mould — and here the mould breaks in two places.**

| Break | The others | **Here** |
|---|---|---|
| **Verb** | *Kadhdhab**at*** (feminine) | ***Kadhdhaba*** (masculine) |
| **Attribution** | *Akhūhum …* ("their brother") | ***absent*** — only *Shuʿaybun* |

**The reason for these two breaks and their connection with each other were treated in Table 2 of the section "The backbone of the sūra"; I do not repeat it.** Its summary: those addressed are named not by a lineage but by a **place** (*aṣḥābu'l-Ayka*), and *aṣḥāb* is a masculine plural.

### ٱلْأَيْكَة — the meaning of the word

The root **أ-ي-ك**: **dense woodland, interwoven thicket.** *Ayka* — a grove whose trees stand thick.

**The word occurs in four places in the Qurʾān** (al-Ḥijr 15/78; al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/176; Ṣād 38/13; Qāf 50/14) **and was mentioned in `038-sad.md` and `050-kaf.md`.**

**And the people of Shuʿayb are named in the Qurʾān by two different names, and this must be recorded:**

| Name | Where | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| ***Midian*** | Al-Aʿrāf 7/85; Hūd 11/84; al-ʿAnkabūt 29/36 | **The name of a people / a town** — *akhūhum Shuʿayb* is said |
| ***Aṣḥābu'l-Ayka*** | Al-Ḥijr 15/78; **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/176**; Ṣād 38/13; Qāf 50/14 | **A place name** — *akhūhum* is not said |

**And what the table shows is an order that can be verified from the text:** where the Qurʾān says **Midian** it says *akhūhum Shuʿayb*; where it says **al-Ayka** it does not.

**Whether these two are the same community or two different ones has been disputed. I give the transmitted views in a table; I do not impose a preference:**

| View | Ground | Its weak side |
|---|---|---|
| **The same community, two names** | In both places there is the same prophet (Shuʿayb) and the same subject (measure and weight) | The difference over *akhūhum* has to be explained |
| **Two different communities, the same prophet** | The Qurʾān's not saying *akhūhum* is counted a mark of distinction; the manners of destruction are also recounted differently (*ṣayḥa* / *rajfa* — Midian; *yawmu'ẓ-ẓulla* — al-Ayka) | The Qurʾān does not openly say that they are two different communities |

**I do not enter into geographical detail the Qurʾān does not give.**

**And as was recorded at `038-sad.md` 38/13, there *aṣḥābu'l-Ayka* is named in a series of destroyed communities and it is said *ulāʾika'l-aḥzāb*.** I rely on the note there.

---

## 26/181-184 — أَوْفُوا۟ ٱلْكَيْلَ وَلَا تَكُونُوا۟ مِنَ ٱلْمُخْسِرِينَ · وَزِنُوا۟ بِٱلْقِسْطَاسِ ٱلْمُسْتَقِيمِ · وَلَا تَبْخَسُوا۟ ٱلنَّاسَ أَشْيَآءَهُمْ وَلَا تَعْثَوْا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مُفْسِدِينَ · وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَكُمْ وَٱلْجِبِلَّةَ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ

*Awfu'l-kayla wa lā takūnū mina'l-mukhsirīn · Wa zinū bi'l-qisṭāsi'l-mustaqīm · Wa lā tabkhasu'n-nāsa ashyāʾahum wa lā taʿthaw fi'l-arḍi mufsidīn · Wa'ttaqu'lladhī khalaqakum wa'l-jibillata'l-awwalīn*

"**Give full measure, and do not be of those who cause loss. · Weigh with the true balance. · And do not undervalue people's goods, and do not go about the earth spreading corruption. · And guard yourselves against Him who created you and the generations before.**"

### Four verses, three prohibitions, one command

**This is the most concrete list of instructions given by a messenger in the sūra. And its field is a single one: trade.**

| Verse | Expression | Kind | Field |
|---|---|---|---|
| **181a** | *Awfu'l-kayla* | **Command** | **The measure** — volume |
| **181b** | *Lā takūnū mina'l-mukhsirīn* | Prohibition | The measure |
| **182** | *Zinū bi'l-qisṭāsi'l-mustaqīm* | **Command** | **The balance** — weight |
| **183a** | *Lā tabkhasu'n-nāsa ashyāʾahum* | Prohibition | **Value** — price |
| **183b** | *Lā taʿthaw fi'l-arḍi mufsidīn* | Prohibition | **General** |

**And the order is worth recording: volume → weight → value → the general order.** The list, that is, begins with two concrete instruments of measure and widens to **price**, and from there to **the order of the earth**.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the arrangement of the four verses:** the shortfall made in measuring is placed, in the language of the verse, not as a fault of trade but as **a matter that touches the order of the earth**.

### ٱلْكَيْل and ٱلْمُخْسِرِين

The root **ك-ي-ل**: **to measure with a measure** (a measure of volume). *Kayl* — the measure and the act of measuring.

The root **خ-س-ر**: **to fall short, to suffer loss.** Form IV (*akhsara*) is transitive: **to cause a shortfall, to cause loss.** *Mukhsir* — one who causes a shortfall.

**And this field was treated in detail in `083-mutaffifin.md` and I rely on that treatment.** The notes there were, in summary, these:

- The root **ط-ف-ف** (*taṭfīf*): to trifle at the edge, at the extremity of a thing — **the small shortfall made in measuring.**
- The verb used at al-Muṭaffifīn 83/3 is also from the root **خ-س-ر**: *wa idhā kālūhum aw wazanūhum **yukhsirūn***.
- And the distinction recorded there: ***bakhs* is the shortfall made in price, *taṭfīf* the shortfall made in quantity.**

**Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/181 and al-Muṭaffifīn 83/3, that is, use the same root.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text. **And in `083-mutaffifin.md` this verse (al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/183) was already cited.**

### ٱلْقِسْطَاسِ ٱلْمُسْتَقِيم — the balance

**قِسْطَاس** — most of the lexicographers derive the word from the root **ق-س-ط** (justice, equity); some record that it may have entered Arabic from another language. **I make no firm choice.**

**Its meaning: the balance, the instrument of weighing.** The word occurs in two places in the Qurʾān: **al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/182** and **al-Isrāʾ 17/35** (*wa zinū bi'l-qisṭāsi'l-mustaqīm* — **exactly the same sentence**).

**This is a datum that can be verified from the text: the same sentence is repeated unchanged in two sūras.**

**ٱلْمُسْتَقِيم** — root **ق-و-م**, the active participle of Form X: **straight, without deviation.** The same word occurs in the construction *ṣirāṭa'l-mustaqīm* (analysed in `001-fatiha.md`).

**And *mīzān* was treated at `057-hadid.md` 57/25 and I rely on that treatment.** The notes there were, in summary, these:

- **Three things are sent down together: the Book, the balance, and iron.** The Book represents **the principle**, the balance **the instrument of measure**, iron **the safeguard**.
- **The case of "the Book present, the balance absent":** the principle is there, the measure is not — everyone's justice in his own head.

**What is to be added here is a link, and I record it as my own reading:** at al-Ḥadīd 57/25 the balance is something **sent down**; at al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/182 the balance is something **used**. **One takes as its subject the source of the measure, the other its use.** And al-Shuʿarāʾ asks for the use from a prophet's mouth in the most everyday field of all: **the market.**

### وَلَا تَبْخَسُوا۟ ٱلنَّاسَ أَشْيَآءَهُمْ — root ب-خ-س

**The root was analysed in `083-mutaffifin.md` and I rely on that treatment.** The description there: **ب-خ-س** — **to take a thing by lowering its value, to give short of what is due.**

**And the distinction recorded there deserves to be repeated here, because Shuʿayb's list contains both:**

| Root | Where the shortfall is made | The verse in al-Shuʿarāʾ |
|---|---|---|
| **خ-س-ر / ط-ف-ف** | **In quantity** — measure and weight | **181** |
| **ب-خ-س** | **In price** — in value | **183** |

**In three verses, then, Shuʿayb closes off three different kinds of cheating: volume, weight, value.** This is an order that can be verified from the text.

**The best-known use of the root in the Qurʾān is in the account of Joseph:** *wa sharawhu bi-thamanin **bakhsin** darāhima maʿdūda* (Yūsuf 12/20) — "and they sold him for a paltry price, a few counted dirhams". **This example was recorded in `083-mutaffifin.md` and `072-cin.md`.**

**And a point of word order must be recorded: *lā tabkhasu'n-nāsa ashyāʾahum*.** The verb takes two objects: **people** and **their goods**. In Arabic this construction announces together **from whom** and **in what** the shortfall is made.

**And the word *ashyāʾ* must be recorded: "things".** The most general word of all. **The prohibition, that is, looks not only to merchandise but to everything a person has a right to.**

**The transmitted explanations support this breadth:** it is said that the word may be understood not only of property but of **right, labour and standing** as well. **I transmit this as a view;** no limit is set in the verse.

### وَلَا تَعْثَوْا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مُفْسِدِين — the fourth prohibition

The root **ع-ث-و / ع-ي-ث**: **to spread extreme corruption, to turn a thing upside down.** The lexicographers record that the word is used for **the most violent kind of corruption**.

**And *mufsidīn* comes as a circumstantial: "as corrupters".** The act itself is already corruption; the circumstantial reinforces it.

**The same sentence is repeated several times in the Qurʾān** (al-Baqara 2/60; al-Aʿrāf 7/74; Hūd 11/85) **and at Hūd 11/85 it occurs from Shuʿayb's mouth in almost the same order as in this verse:** *wa yā qawmi awfu'l-mikyāla wa'l-mīzāna bi'l-qisṭi wa lā tabkhasu'n-nāsa ashyāʾahum wa lā taʿthaw fi'l-arḍi mufsidīn.*

**This is an overlap that can be verified from the text: the two sūras give the same triad in the same order.**

### وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَكُمْ وَٱلْجِبِلَّةَ ٱلْأَوَّلِين — the setting of the ground

**And when the list is finished, a ground is set under the whole list: the creator.**

**جِبِلَّة** — root **ج-ب-ل**: the sense the lexicographers give is **creation, constitution; a community created in a certain state.** From the same root: *jabal* (mountain) and *jibilla* (constitution, nature). **The word here means "generations, communities".**

**In this pattern the word occurs only here in the Qurʾān;** another derivative occurs at Yā Sīn 36/62: *wa laqad aḍalla minkum **jibillan** kathīrā*.

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the ground is extended into the past.** *Khalaqakum **wa'l-jibillata'l-awwalīn*** — "who created you **and the generations before**".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the parallel sentence in the ʿĀd section:** Hūd too had used the same structure — *wa'ttaqu'lladhī **amaddakum** bimā taʿlamūn* (132). **Both messengers, after saying "guard yourselves against God", add a relative clause.**

| Account | Expression | Which verb |
|---|---|---|
| **Hūd 26/132** | *Wa'ttaqu'lladhī **amaddakum** bimā taʿlamūn* | **Giving** — favour |
| **Shuʿayb 26/184** | *Wa'ttaqu'lladhī **khalaqakum** wa'l-jibillata'l-awwalīn* | **Creating** — existence |

**This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.** And the difference is meaningful: **ʿĀd is reminded of favour, al-Ayka of existence.**

**And a link can be made with the seventy-sixth verse:** Abraham too, in answer to the proof from the forefathers, had said *wa ābāʾukumu'l-**aqdamūn***. **The sūra reaches back to "those who came before" in four places: 76 (*al-aqdamūn*), 137 (*al-awwalīn*), 184 (*al-awwalīn*), 196 (*zuburi'l-awwalīn*).** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

---

## 26/185-191 — قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّمَآ أَنتَ مِنَ ٱلْمُسَحَّرِينَ · وَمَآ أَنتَ إِلَّا بَشَرٌ مِّثْلُنَا وَإِن نَّظُنُّكَ لَمِنَ ٱلْكَٰذِبِينَ · فَأَسْقِطْ عَلَيْنَا كِسَفًا مِّنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ إِن كُنتَ مِنَ ٱلصَّٰدِقِينَ · قَالَ رَبِّىٓ أَعْلَمُ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ · فَكَذَّبُوهُ فَأَخَذَهُمْ عَذَابُ يَوْمِ ٱلظُّلَّةِ ۚ إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ عَذَابَ يَوْمٍ عَظِيمٍ · إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَءَايَةً ۖ وَمَا كَانَ أَكْثَرُهُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ · وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

*Qālū innamā anta mina'l-musaḥḥarīn · Wa mā anta illā basharun mithlunā wa in naẓunnuka la-mina'l-kādhibīn · Fa-asqiṭ ʿalaynā kisafan mina's-samāʾi in kunta mina'ṣ-ṣādiqīn · Qāla rabbī aʿlamu bimā taʿmalūn · Fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-akhadhahum ʿadhābu yawmi'ẓ-ẓulla; innahu kāna ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm · Inna fī dhālika la-āyatan wa mā kāna aktharuhum muʾminīn · Wa inna rabbaka la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'r-raḥīm*

"They said: '**You are only one of the bewitched. · You are nothing but a mortal like us; and we think you are one of the liars. · Drop fragments of the sky upon us, if you are one of the truthful!**' · He said: '**My Lord knows best what you do.**' · **They denied him, and thereupon the punishment of the day of the shadow seized them. It was indeed the punishment of a great day.**' · In this there is surely a sign; but most of them were not believers. · And your Lord — He indeed — is the Mighty, the Merciful."

### Thamūd and al-Ayka — a comparison of the two answers

**The answers of the two peoples are almost the same, and their differences must be recorded:**

| | **Thamūd 153-154** | **Al-Ayka 185-187** |
|---|---|---|
| **First sentence** | *Innamā anta mina'l-musaḥḥarīn* | *Innamā anta mina'l-musaḥḥarīn* — **the same** |
| **Second sentence** | *Mā anta illā basharun mithlunā* | *Wa mā anta illā basharun mithlunā* — **the same** |
| **Addition** | **none** | *Wa in naẓunnuka la-mina'l-kādhibīn* |
| **Demand** | *Fa'ti bi-āyatin* — "bring a sign" | *Fa-asqiṭ ʿalaynā kisafan mina's-samāʾ* — **"drop a fragment of the sky"** |

**There are two differences, and both look in the same direction: al-Ayka's answer goes further.**

**One — an accusation has been added: *la-mina'l-kādhibīn* ("one of the liars").**

**Two — the demand has changed.** Thamūd was asking for a **sign**; al-Ayka asks directly for **punishment**.

**I record this as an observation:** when the answers of the five peoples in the sūra are set in order, the demands grow steadily harsher. **And what the last people ask for is what will be given to them** — they said *fa-asqiṭ ʿalaynā*, and *fa-akhadhahum ʿadhābu yawmi'ẓ-ẓulla* came about.

**And the principle recorded in `085-buruc.md` is at work here too: the outcome is announced in the language of the demand.**

### كِسَفًا مِّنَ ٱلسَّمَآء — fragments of the sky

The root **ك-س-ف**: **to break into pieces, to cut.** *Kisaf* — the plural of *kisfa*: **pieces, sections.**

**The word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān, and always together with the sky** (al-Isrāʾ 17/92; al-Rūm 30/48; Sabaʾ 34/9; al-Ṭūr 52/44 — treated in `052-tur.md`).

**And the verse recorded at `052-tur.md` 52/44 attaches here:** *wa in yaraw kisfan mina's-samāʾi sāqiṭan yaqūlū saḥābun markūm* — "if they saw a fragment of the sky falling they would say, 'piled-up clouds'". **The Qurʾān, that is, records in another sūra that even when the thing asked for is given it will be interpreted otherwise.**

**I record this link as a reading of the Qurʾān by the Qurʾān, and it stands on the same line as the fourth verse of the sūra.**

### قَالَ رَبِّىٓ أَعْلَمُ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ — the brevity of the answer

**And Shuʿayb's answer is the shortest of all the messengers' answers in the sūra: five words.**

**And its content must be recorded: the answer neither refuses nor discusses the demand.** They asked for punishment; Shuʿayb says "my Lord knows best what you do". **He takes the matter away from himself, that is, and leaves it to knowledge and judgement.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is its overlap with Noah's answer:** Noah too did not issue the judgement asked of him, but said *in ḥisābuhum illā ʿalā rabbī* (113). **Both messengers end the dispute by saying that the judgement is not theirs.**

| Verse | Who | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **112-113** | **Noah** | *Wa mā ʿilmī bimā kānū yaʿmalūn · in ḥisābuhum illā ʿalā rabbī* |
| **188** | **Shuʿayb** | *Rabbī **aʿlamu bimā taʿmalūn*** |

**Both sentences use the same roots (ع-ل-م and ع-م-ل).** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And the difference must be recorded:** Noah says *"I have no knowledge"*, Shuʿayb says *"my Lord knows better"*. **Both draw the same limit, but from different sides.**

### يَوْمِ ٱلظُّلَّة — the day of the shadow

The root **ظ-ل-ل**: **shade.** *Ẓulla* — **a shade, a thing that covers over.** The word is used in the Qurʾān both of a protecting shade (*ẓulalun mina'n-nār* — reversed at al-Zumar 39/16) and of cloud (*ẓulalin mina'l-ghamām* — al-Baqara 2/210).

**And the Qurʾān does not explain what this punishment was.** *Yawmu'ẓ-ẓulla* — "the day of the shadow" is said; what the shadow was is not.

**In the reports a cloud, heat and fire are spoken of; these are not in the Qurʾān. I do not enter into a detail the Qurʾān does not give.**

**There are two things to be recorded, and both are read off the text:**

**One — the word is a word that suggests protection.** In a hot climate shade is what one takes refuge in. **And the punishment is named with that word.**

**Two — a link can be made with the name of the people.** *Aṣḥābu'l-**Ayka*** — "the people of the dense thicket". **A thicket is the source of shade.**

**I record this link as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the people are named as the people of a shaded place, and their punishment comes by way of a shade. **That this is intended is not stated in the text;** that the two words stand in the same sūra fifteen verses apart is a datum of the text. **And it accords with the principle recorded in `085-buruc.md`: the sentence is announced in the language of the offence or of the condition.**

### إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ عَذَابَ يَوْمٍ عَظِيم — the third occurrence

**The construction occurs three times in the sūra (135, 156, 189), and all three are in three different accounts.** This was tabulated in the section on 26/131-135.

**And a point of word order must be recorded: in the first two occurrences the construction stood inside a *warning*** (*innī akhāfu ʿalaykum ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm*; *fa-yaʾkhudhakum ʿadhābu yawmin ʿaẓīm*). **In the third it stands inside a *statement of fact*:** *innahu kāna ʿadhāba yawmin ʿaẓīm.*

| Verse | Who says it | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **135** | **Hūd** | **Fear** — it has not yet happened |
| **156** | **Ṣāliḥ** | **Condition** — if it happens |
| **189** | **The narrator** | **Statement** — it happened |

**This is an order that can be verified from the text: the same construction occurs three times, moving from warning to fulfilment.**

### The sealing of the account — 190-191

**The eighth and last seal of the sūra. The mould was treated at 26/8-9.**

**And an observation about position:** the seal here closes not only the account of Shuʿayb but **the whole seven-account sequence**. The next verse will change the subject and turn to the one addressed: *wa innahu la-tanzīlu rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* (192).

**And the construction *rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* must be recorded:** five times in five accounts it was said "my wage falls only upon **the Lord of all the worlds**". **In the hundred and ninety-second verse the same construction will be used of the source of the Qurʾān.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text, and it binds the two sections together.

---

## 26/192-195 — وَإِنَّهُۥ لَتَنزِيلُ رَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ · نَزَلَ بِهِ ٱلرُّوحُ ٱلْأَمِينُ · عَلَىٰ قَلْبِكَ لِتَكُونَ مِنَ ٱلْمُنذِرِينَ · بِلِسَانٍ عَرَبِىٍّ مُّبِينٍ

*Wa innahu la-tanzīlu rabbi'l-ʿālamīn · Nazala bihi'r-rūḥu'l-amīn · ʿAlā qalbika li-takūna mina'l-mundhirīn · Bi-lisānin ʿarabiyyin mubīn*

"**It is surely the sending down of the Lord of all the worlds. · The trustworthy Spirit brought it down — · upon your heart, that you might be one of the warners, · in a clear Arabic tongue.**"

### Four verses, one sentence

**Grammatically the four verses are a single structure, and each verse adds an element:**

| Verse | Element | Answers the question |
|---|---|---|
| **192** | *La-tanzīlu rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* | **From whom?** |
| **193** | *Nazala bihi'r-rūḥu'l-amīn* | **Who brought it?** |
| **194** | *ʿAlā qalbika li-takūna mina'l-mundhirīn* | **Where and why?** |
| **195** | *Bi-lisānin ʿarabiyyin mubīn* | **In what language?** |

**The four verses thus give the chain of revelation from beginning to end: the source, the carrier, the destination, the purpose, the form.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** through seven accounts the sūra recounted what the messengers said; **now it recounts where this speech comes from.**

### تَنزِيل — the verbal noun

The root **ن-ز-ل**: to descend. Form II (*nazzala*) announces **gradual descent** or **intensification**. *Tanzīl* is its verbal noun.

**And a point of word order: the sentence does not say *innahu munazzalun* ("it has been sent down"); it says *la-tanzīlu rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* — "the sending down of the Lord of all the worlds".** Not a passive participle, that is, but **a verbal noun**.

**In Arabic making a verbal noun the predicate announces intensification:** not "it is a thing sent down" but **"it is the very act of sending down."**

**The same mould is the opening of several sūras of the Qurʾān:** *tanzīlu'l-kitābi mina'llāhi'l-ʿazīzi'l-ḥakīm* (al-Zumar 39/1; al-Jāthiya 45/2; al-Aḥqāf 46/2 — treated in `046-ahkaf.md`), *tanzīlan mimman khalaqa'l-arḍa* (Ṭāhā 20/4), *tanzīlu'l-ʿazīzi'r-raḥīm* (Yā Sīn 36/5 — treated in `036-yasin.md`).

**And Yā Sīn 36/5 is worth recording: there the two names to which *tanzīl* is attributed are the same as the two names in the seal of al-Shuʿarāʾ — *al-ʿAzīzu'r-Raḥīm*.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

### نَزَلَ بِهِ ٱلرُّوحُ ٱلْأَمِين — the carrier

**And the verb must be recorded: *nazala bihi* — "he came down with it".**

**In Arabic *nazala bi-* means "to bring a thing down"** ("to descend bringing it along"). **The Spirit, that is, is recounted not as the party who *sends* the revelation but as the party who *brings* it.**

**Among the variant readings the reading *nazzala bihi* (Form II) is also transmitted;** the difference of sense is not great.

**ٱلرُّوحُ ٱلْأَمِين** — "the trustworthy Spirit".

**Who is meant is not said in the verse; but the intra-Qurʾānic evidence is strong and was tabulated in `097-kadr.md` and `078-nebe.md`. I rely on the note there:**

| The expression in the Qurʾān | Place |
|---|---|
| *Nazala bihi'r-**rūḥu'l-amīn*** | **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/193** |
| *Nazzalahu **rūḥu'l-qudus*** | Al-Naḥl 16/102 |
| *Fa-innahu nazzalahu ʿalā qalbika bi-idhni'llāh* — the subject is **Gabriel (Jibrīl)** | Al-Baqara 2/97 |

**Read together, the three verses yield three names doing the same work, and in the third the name is given openly: Gabriel.** This is a chain that can be verified from the text; **it was recorded in `097-kadr.md` as the view "with the strongest intra-Qurʾānic evidence".**

**And the attribute *amīn* must be recorded.** In the sūra five messengers had introduced themselves with this attribute: *innī lakum rasūlun **amīn*** (107, 125, 143, 162, 178).

| Who | Attribute | Verses |
|---|---|---|
| **The five messengers** | *amīn* | 107, 125, 143, 162, 178 |
| **The one who brings the revelation** | *al-amīn* | **193** |

**The sūra thus uses the attribute of the carrier five times of human messengers and once of an angel.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And as my own reading I record this:** *amāna* is to deliver a thing as it is — neither taking away nor adding. **This is the whole claim of the sūra:** the speech has come from its source to the one addressed **unchanged**. **And that both links in the chain are named with the same attribute carries that claim.**

**And the other side will be described at exactly this point in verse 223:** *yulqūna's-samʿa wa aktharuhum kādhibūn* — "they lend an ear, and most of them are liars."

### عَلَىٰ قَلْبِكَ — the destination

**And a point of word order must be recorded: the place the revelation comes down to is said to be not "to you" but "to your *heart*".**

**The root ق-ل-ب** was analysed in the fiftieth verse (*munqalibūn*): **to turn a thing over; the thing that turns most from state to state.**

**The transmitted explanations:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| **The heart is the centre of grasping and memory** | The Qurʾān attributes reasoning and understanding to the heart (*lahum qulūbun lā yafqahūna bihā* — al-Aʿrāf 7/179) |
| **It is where the revelation is memorised and preserved** | The same expression occurs at al-Baqara 2/97: *nazzalahu ʿalā qalbika* |
| **The messenger carries the revelation not as an external object but within himself** | The direct sense of the expression |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And a link within the sūra must be recorded:** in the eighty-ninth verse the measure was the soundness of the *heart* (*illā man ata'llāha bi-**qalbin** salīm*). **In the hundred and ninety-fourth verse the place the revelation comes down to is again the heart.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

**لِتَكُونَ مِنَ ٱلْمُنذِرِين** — a clause of purpose.

**And the mould must be recorded: not "that you might be a warner" but "that you might be one of the warners".** The "assignment to a group" mould that occurs often in the sūra (tabulated at 26/29-31) is used here of the one addressed himself.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the sūra as a whole:** through seven accounts seven warners were recounted. **The hundred and ninety-fourth verse places the one addressed inside that series.** And the sentence recorded at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/9 stands on the same line: *qul mā kuntu bidʿan mina'r-rusul* — "I am not the first of the messengers".

### بِلِسَانٍ عَرَبِىٍّ مُّبِين — the note on language

**The root ع-ر-ب** was analysed at `056-vakia.md` 56/37 and I rely on that treatment. The description there: **the core sense of the root is "to express clearly and plainly, to set out one's meaning without hiding it".** *Aʿraba ʿani'sh-shayʾ* — he made a thing known plainly. *Iʿrāb* — bringing out into the open; **the grammatical term *iʿrāb* also comes from here: a word's bringing out its function in the sentence through the vowel at its end.**

**And the same word was treated at `043-zuhruf.md` 43/3 (*innā jaʿalnāhu qurʾānan ʿarabiyyan laʿallakum taʿqilūn*) and I rely on that treatment.**

**What is to be added here is the construction itself: *lisānin ʿarabiyyin mubīn*.**

**The two words come from the same semantic field:** *ʿarabī* (clear) and *mubīn* (making clear). **The construction thus stands as though it were a repetition.**

**The transmitted explanations:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| ***ʿArabī* is the name of the language, *mubīn* its attribute** | The most widespread reading: "the Arabic tongue, which is clear" |
| **Both look to clarity; there is emphasis** | The sense of the root |
| ***Mubīn* qualifies not the language but the Qurʾān** | A remote reading |

**And the five occurrences of the word in the sūra were tabulated in the section on 26/1-2.** The sūra opens with *al-kitābi'l-mubīn* and here ties it to language with *lisānin ʿarabiyyin mubīn*. **This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.**

**And the word *lisān* must be recorded: it occurs in the sūra in three places.**

| Verse | Expression | Who / what |
|---|---|---|
| **13** | *Wa lā yanṭaliqu **lisānī*** | **Moses** — his tongue does not loosen |
| **84** | *Wa'jʿal lī **lisāna** ṣidqin fi'l-ākhirīn* | **Abraham** — a tongue of truth |
| **195** | *Bi-**lisānin** ʿarabiyyin mubīn* | **The Qurʾān** — the language it came down in |

**Three occurrences, three different senses: the organ, being remembered, a language.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text. **And as my own reading I record this:** the sūra had recounted an account that opens with a tongue that does not loosen; **it ends by defining itself as "a clear tongue".** That this link is intended is not stated in the text.

---

## 26/196-199 — وَإِنَّهُۥ لَفِى زُبُرِ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ · أَوَلَمْ يَكُن لَّهُمْ ءَايَةً أَن يَعْلَمَهُۥ عُلَمَٰٓؤُا۟ بَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ · وَلَوْ نَزَّلْنَٰهُ عَلَىٰ بَعْضِ ٱلْأَعْجَمِينَ · فَقَرَأَهُۥ عَلَيْهِم مَّا كَانُوا۟ بِهِۦ مُؤْمِنِينَ

*Wa innahu la-fī zuburi'l-awwalīn · A-wa lam yakun lahum āyatan an yaʿlamahu ʿulamāʾu banī Isrāʾīl · Wa law nazzalnāhu ʿalā baʿḍi'l-aʿjamīn · Fa-qaraʾahu ʿalayhim mā kānū bihi muʾminīn*

"**It is surely in the scriptures of those who came before. · Is it not a sign for them that the learned of the Children of Israel know it? · Had We sent it down upon one of the non-Arabic speakers · and had he recited it to them, they would still not have believed in it.**"

### زُبُر — the meaning of the word

The root **ز-ب-ر**: the core the lexicographers give is **to make firm, to build by laying stone upon stone** (*zabartu'l-biʾr* — I lined the well with stone). **And from there it passes to the sense of "to write":** *zabūr* — what is written, a book. *Zubur* is its plural.

**The link the lexicographers make is this: writing too is a kind of building** — it is set out letter by letter, line by line, and made to last.

**Across the series the root occurred at `054-kamer.md` 54/43, 54/52 and at `035-fatir.md` 35/25.** **Something recorded in `035-fatir.md` attaches here:** there three words were set in order — *bayyināt* (clear proofs), *zubur* (pages), *al-kitābu'l-munīr* (the illuminating Book) — and it was recorded that **"what came is named both as spoken proof and as written document"**. **I rely on that treatment.**

### وَإِنَّهُۥ لَفِى زُبُرِ ٱلْأَوَّلِين — the referent of the pronoun

**And the disputed point of the sentence is this: what does the pronoun *innahu* refer to?**

| Reading | What the pronoun refers to | Its meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **To the Qurʾān** | The same pronoun as in *wa **innahu** la-tanzīl* (192) | "The news of this Book is in the earlier books" |
| **To what the Qurʾān announces** | To the content | "The truths it recounts are in the earlier ones too" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** What is to be recorded is this: **the sentence does not say "the same text is there".** The expression *fī zuburi'l-awwalīn* — "it **is** in the scriptures of those who came before" — can cover both being present as it is and having been announced.

### أَوَلَمْ يَكُن لَّهُمْ ءَايَةً — the kind of the proof

**And a point of word order must be recorded: here the word *āya* occurs once more and in a new sense.**

**In the sūra *āya* has been used in three senses:**

| Sense | Where |
|---|---|
| **A miracle, a sign coming down from the sky** | 4, 154 |
| **An erected marker, a monument** | 128 |
| **A lesson, a proof** | In the eight seals; **197** |

**This is a datum that can be verified from the text.**

**And what is presented as proof here must be recorded: not a miracle but a piece of *knowledge*.** *An yaʿlamahu ʿulamāʾu banī Isrāʾīl* — "that the learned of the Children of Israel know it".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verse itself:** those addressed were expecting a miracle of the kind recounted in the fourth verse. **The verse points to a proof standing in front of them: the knowledge of the people of the Book who came before them.**

**And the same proof occurs at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/10:** *wa shahida shāhidun min banī Isrāʾīla ʿalā mithlihi fa-āmana wa'stakbartum* — "and a witness from the Children of Israel testified to the like of it and believed, while you grew proud." **It was treated there and I rely on that treatment.**

**And the frame recorded at `046-ahkaf.md` 46/4 attaches here:** there two kinds of proof were asked for — **observation** (*arūnī mādhā khalaqū*) and **transmission** (*iʾtūnī bi-kitābin min qabli hādhā aw athāratin min ʿilm*). **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/196-197 offers a proof of the second kind.**

**A note on the variant readings:** the readings *āyatan* (accusative) and *āyatun* (nominative) are transmitted for the verse; **both come to the same meaning, only the subject and predicate of the sentence change places.**

### ٱلْأَعْجَمِين — root ع-ج-م

The root **ع-ج-م**: the core the lexicographers give is **obscurity, unintelligibility** — that is, the exact opposite of the root **ع-ر-ب**. *ʿUjma* — obscurity in speech; *aʿjam* — **one who cannot set out his meaning plainly.**

**And two uses of the word must be distinguished:**

| Word | Its meaning |
|---|---|
| ***Aʿjamī*** | **One who does not know Arabic, whose speech is not understood** — an attribute of the person |
| ***ʿAjamī*** | Not an Arab — an attribute of lineage |

**The *al-aʿjamīn* of the verse is in the first sense: those whose speech is not understood.** The measure, that is, is not lineage but **intelligibility**.

**This distinction must be recorded, because this is where a judgement would be built:** the verse takes as its subject not a nation but **a linguistic situation**. **This accords with the principle recorded in `STYLE.md`.**

### A comparison with Fuṣṣilat 41/44

**The same supposition is set up once more in the Qurʾān and was treated at `041-fussilet.md` 41/44. I rely on that treatment.**

**Fuṣṣilat 41/44:** *wa law jaʿalnāhu qurʾānan aʿjamiyyan la-qālū law lā fuṣṣilat āyātuhu; a-aʿjamiyyun wa ʿarabiyyun* — "Had We made it a Qurʾān in a foreign tongue they would certainly have said: 'Should its verses not have been made clear? A foreign tongue and an Arab?'"

**The two verses set up the same supposition, but from different sides. The comparison is in the table:**

| | **Fuṣṣilat 41/44** | **Al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/198-199** |
|---|---|---|
| **The supposition** | *Law **jaʿalnāhu** qurʾānan aʿjamiyyan* | *Law **nazzalnāhu** ʿalā baʿḍi'l-aʿjamīn* |
| **What changes** | **The language of the Book** — if the text were foreign | **The language of the messenger** — if the carrier were foreign |
| **The reaction of those addressed** | *La-qālū law lā fuṣṣilat āyātuh* — **they would object** | *Mā kānū bihi muʾminīn* — **they would still not believe** |
| **What it shows** | **That the objection is a pretext** — whatever the language, an objection will be found | **That the reason for not believing is not language** |
| **Mould** | Condition + **the words that would be said** | Condition + **the outcome** |

**What the two verses build together must be recorded, and this is my own reading:**

**Fuṣṣilat states in advance the *objection* of those addressed: "had it been in a foreign tongue they would have said 'it cannot be understood'."** **Al-Shuʿarāʾ does not even name the objection; it states the outcome directly: "they would still not believe."**

**The two verses come out at the same door by two different roads:** one shows **that the pretext changes its place**, the other **that the pretext does not change the outcome**.

**And something can be added here to what was recorded in `041-fussilet.md`:** in the supposition in al-Shuʿarāʾ what changes is **not the Book but the person.** *ʿAlā baʿḍi'l-aʿjamīn* — "upon **one** of the non-Arabic speakers". And the verb has been added too: *fa-**qaraʾahu** ʿalayhim* — "and had he **recited** it to them". **The supposition contains the act of reciting as well: had the text come, had it been recited, they would still not have believed.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the verse carries the possibility to its very end and leaves the outcome the same at every stage.

### What this says to the present

The measure the two verses build together serves to test whether the grounds put forward in a dispute are the real reason. **The test is simple: if the ground were removed, would the position change?**

**The verse makes this test by a supposition** — if the language had been different, if the carrier had been different. **And its answer is tied to the same place as the third verse of the sūra:** the messenger's grief is needless, because the matter **is not an inadequacy of expression**.

---

## 26/200-209 — كَذَٰلِكَ سَلَكْنَٰهُ فِى قُلُوبِ ٱلْمُجْرِمِينَ · لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِهِۦ حَتَّىٰ يَرَوُا۟ ٱلْعَذَابَ ٱلْأَلِيمَ · فَيَأْتِيَهُم بَغْتَةً وَهُمْ لَا يَشْعُرُونَ · فَيَقُولُوا۟ هَلْ نَحْنُ مُنظَرُونَ · أَفَبِعَذَابِنَا يَسْتَعْجِلُونَ · أَفَرَءَيْتَ إِن مَّتَّعْنَٰهُمْ سِنِينَ · ثُمَّ جَآءَهُم مَّا كَانُوا۟ يُوعَدُونَ · مَآ أَغْنَىٰ عَنْهُم مَّا كَانُوا۟ يُمَتَّعُونَ · وَمَآ أَهْلَكْنَا مِن قَرْيَةٍ إِلَّا لَهَا مُنذِرُونَ · ذِكْرَىٰ وَمَا كُنَّا ظَٰلِمِينَ

*Kadhālika salaknāhu fī qulūbi'l-mujrimīn · Lā yuʾminūna bihi ḥattā yarawu'l-ʿadhāba'l-alīm · Fa-yaʾtiyahum baghtatan wa hum lā yashʿurūn · Fa-yaqūlū hal naḥnu munẓarūn · A-fa-bi-ʿadhābinā yastaʿjilūn · A-fa-raʾayta in mattaʿnāhum sinīn · Thumma jāʾahum mā kānū yūʿadūn · Mā aghnā ʿanhum mā kānū yumattaʿūn · Wa mā ahlaknā min qaryatin illā lahā mundhirūn · Dhikrā wa mā kunnā ẓālimīn*

"**Thus have We threaded it into the hearts of the guilty. · They will not believe in it until they see the painful punishment. · It will come upon them suddenly, and they will not be aware. · Then they will say: 'Are we to be given respite?' · Do they then seek to hasten Our punishment? · Tell me, then: if We let them enjoy themselves for years, · and then what they were promised came to them, · of what use to them would be what they were allowed to enjoy? · And We have destroyed no township that did not have its warners — · as a reminder. And We were never unjust.**"

### سَلَكْنَٰهُ فِى قُلُوبِ ٱلْمُجْرِمِين — the dispute over the pronoun

The root **س-ل-ك**: **to pass a thing through a way or a hole, to thread.** Its concrete use: to pass a thread through the eye of a needle. The Qurʾān uses the same root in the form *naslukuhu fī qulūbi'l-mujrimīn* (al-Ḥijr 15/12) as well — **almost the same sentence.**

**And the disputed point is this: what does the pronoun of *salaknāhu* refer to?**

| Reading | The pronoun | Its meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **To the Qurʾān** | The same as the *innahu* pronouns in the preceding verses | "We threaded the Qurʾān into their hearts — yet they do not believe" |
| **To the denial, the rejection** | To the shared outcome of the preceding accounts | "We threaded this attitude into their hearts" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** What is to be recorded is this: **on the first reading the emphasis is that the speech has reached them but found no response; on the second, that the attitude is an outcome.**

**And the word *kadhālika* ("thus") must be recorded:** the word attaches to the preceding verses. **It comes, that is, after the supposition of verses 198-199 ("even if a foreigner recited it they would not believe").** This is a contextual datum that supports the first reading.

**ٱلْمُجْرِمِين** — the root was analysed in the ninety-ninth verse (ج-ر-م — to pluck fruit, and from there to earn guilt); **I do not repeat it.**

### حَتَّىٰ يَرَوُا۟ ٱلْعَذَابَ ٱلْأَلِيم — the setting of the limit

**And the sentence sets a limit on the time of belief: *ḥattā yarawu'l-ʿadhāb*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the link with 26/4:** in the fourth verse it was said *"if We willed We would send down a sign from heaven and their necks would bow"*. **In the two hundred and first verse it is said that they will believe when they see the punishment.** The two verses say the same thing: **when a compelling sign comes, no resistance remains** — but that belief is not what counts as belief.

**And the Qurʾān says this openly elsewhere:** *fa-lam yaku yanfaʿuhum īmānuhum lammā raʾaw baʾsanā* (al-Muʾmin 40/85 — treated in `040-gafir.md`).

### بَغْتَةً وَهُمْ لَا يَشْعُرُون — suddenness

The root **ب-غ-ت**: **to catch suddenly and unprepared.**

**And the addition of *wa hum lā yashʿurūn* must be recorded:** *baghtatan* already announces suddenness; on top of it "while they are not aware" is added. **In Arabic this is reinforcement.**

The root **ش-ع-ر**: **to perceive what is fine and hidden.** The root was mentioned in the hundred and thirteenth verse (*law tashʿurūn*) and **will return by name in the final section of the sūra: *al-shuʿarāʾ*** (224). **This is a repetition that can be verified from the text, and the full analysis of the root will be made there.**

**I record an observation:** the sense of the root is **"to perceive"**. And in saying that the punishment comes **unperceived**, the verse uses this root. **Twenty-four verses later the sūra will speak of a group that takes its name from the same root.**

### هَلْ نَحْنُ مُنظَرُون — the request for respite

The root **ن-ظ-ر**: **to look; and to wait, to grant a term.** *Inẓār* — the granting of respite. *Munẓar* — a passive participle: **one granted respite.**

**And the word must not be confused with *mundhir* (a warner — from the root ن-ذ-ر); they are two different roots.** Both occur in the sūra: *munẓarūn* (203) and *mundhirūn* (208).

**And a point of word order must be recorded: what is asked for is not an opportunity to believe but *time*.**

### أَفَبِعَذَابِنَا يَسْتَعْجِلُون — the demand to hasten

**And an opposition is set up between two verses:**

| Verse | The demand | When |
|---|---|---|
| **203** | *Hal naḥnu munẓarūn* — **"give us respite"** | **When the punishment comes** |
| **204** | *A-fa-bi-ʿadhābinā **yastaʿjilūn*** — **"they seek to hasten it"** | **Before the punishment** |

**The same people, at two different times, two opposite demands.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And the demand of the people of al-Ayka attaches to this verse:** *fa-asqiṭ ʿalaynā kisafan mina's-samāʾ* (187) — "drop a fragment of the sky". **The sūra, that is, applies here to those addressed a scene it recounted twenty verses before.**

The root **ع-ج-ل**: **to hasten.** Form X (*istaʿjala*) — **to ask for haste, to demand that a thing be hastened.**

### أَفَرَءَيْتَ إِن مَّتَّعْنَٰهُمْ سِنِين — a question across three verses

**After the hundred and ninety-fifth verse the sūra sets up a question to think on, and the question spreads across three verses (205-207).**

| Verse | Element |
|---|---|
| **205** | **Condition** — "if We let them enjoy for years" |
| **206** | **Second condition** — "and then what was promised came" |
| **207** | **Answer** — "of what use would be what they enjoyed?" |

**And the word *sinīn* (years) must be recorded:** the term is set long. **The question says not "if it lasts a short time" but "even if it lasts a long time".**

The root **م-ت-ع**: **to derive benefit from a thing, to enjoy it for a time.** *Matāʿ* — a passing benefit, a provision. **At the core of the word lies transience**, and this was recorded in many sections of the series.

**And the verb occurs twice: *mattaʿnāhum* (205), *yumattaʿūn* (207).** The same root, one active and one passive.

**And at `037-saffat.md` 37/148 the same root closed an account:** *fa-mattaʿnāhum ilā ḥīn* — "so We let them enjoy for a time". **The same mould, in two sūras.**

**م-ا أَغْنَىٰ عَنْهُم** — root **غ-ن-ي**: **to be free of need, to suffice.** *Mā aghnā ʿanhu* — "it warded nothing off from him, it did not suffice him".

**And the mood of the sentence is perfect: *mā aghnā*.** That the apodosis of a conditional comes in the perfect is exactly the technique recorded in the fourth verse: **to recount as already done what is certain to happen.**

### وَمَآ أَهْلَكْنَا مِن قَرْيَةٍ إِلَّا لَهَا مُنذِرُون — the setting down of the rule

**And the sūra states in a single sentence the rule that emerges from the seven accounts.**

**A point of word order must be recorded: the sentence is built with the *mā … illā* mould** — a rule without exception. **And the *min* in *min qaryatin*, coming after a negative, announces comprehensiveness: "no township whatever".**

**And *mundhirūn* is plural.** The singular (*mundhirun*) could also have been said. **The transmitted explanation: that more than one warner may have come to each township, or that the class is meant.**

**And the word ties to the hundred and seventy-third verse:** *fa-sāʾa maṭaru'l-**mundharīn*** — "the rain of **those who were warned**". **The same root, two different voices: there those warned, here those who warn.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

**And at `035-fatir.md` 35/24 the same rule is set down with another word:** *wa in min ummatin illā khalā fīhā nadhīr* — "there is no community in which a warner has not passed". **It was treated there and I rely on that treatment.**

### ذِكْرَىٰ وَمَا كُنَّا ظَٰلِمِين — a ground in two words

**ذِكْرَىٰ** — root **ذ-ك-ر**: to remember, to call to mind. *Dhikrā* — **a reminder.**

**A point of word order:** the word stands alone and can be attached grammatically in several ways (as an object of purpose, *mafʿūl lahu* — "so that it be a reminder"; as a circumstantial — "as a reminder"). **The sense does not change: the reason the warners are sent.**

**وَمَا كُنَّا ظَٰلِمِين** — "and We were never unjust."

**And the work of the sentence must be recorded: this is a note set upon all the accounts of destruction in the sūra.** Through seven accounts peoples were destroyed. **The two hundred and ninth verse says that this was not an injustice — and its ground is in the verse before it: warning had been given.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the two verses together build an argument. **Punishment without warning would be an injustice; because warning was given, it is not.**

**And a similar note occurs at `050-kaf.md` 50/29:** *wa mā anā bi-ẓallāmin li'l-ʿabīd.* **It was treated there.**

---

## 26/210-213 — وَمَا تَنَزَّلَتْ بِهِ ٱلشَّيَٰطِينُ · وَمَا يَنۢبَغِى لَهُمْ وَمَا يَسْتَطِيعُونَ · إِنَّهُمْ عَنِ ٱلسَّمْعِ لَمَعْزُولُونَ · فَلَا تَدْعُ مَعَ ٱللَّهِ إِلَٰهًا ءَاخَرَ فَتَكُونَ مِنَ ٱلْمُعَذَّبِينَ

*Wa mā tanazzalat bihi'sh-shayāṭīn · Wa mā yanbaghī lahum wa mā yastaṭīʿūn · Innahum ʿani's-samʿi la-maʿzūlūn · Fa-lā tadʿu maʿa'llāhi ilāhan ākhara fa-takūna mina'l-muʿadhdhabīn*

"**The satans did not bring it down. · It is not for them to do, nor are they able. · For they are kept far from the hearing.** · **So do not call upon another god beside God, or you will be among those punished.**"

### The refutation of the claim — three stages

**These three verses take up the second claim the sūra answers: "the satans are bringing this speech to him".**

**And the refutation has three stages:**

| Verse | Refutation | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| **210** | *Mā tanazzalat bihi'sh-shayāṭīn* | **The fact** — they did not bring it |
| **211a** | *Wa mā yanbaghī lahum* | **Fitness** — it does not befit them, it is not their business |
| **211b** | *Wa mā yastaṭīʿūn* | **Power** — they cannot do it |
| **212** | *Innahum ʿani's-samʿi la-maʿzūlūn* | **The cause** — they cannot reach the source |

**The refutation descends, that is, from the fact to the cause: they did not bring it → they cannot bring it → because they cannot reach.**

**I record this as an observation about the structure:** the sentence does not merely deny the claim; **it gives the ground of its impossibility as well.**

### تَنَزَّلَتْ — the choice of the verb

**Form V (*tanazzala*): a gradual descent of itself.** And the verb occurs three times in the sūra:

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **210** | *Wa mā **tanazzalat** bihi'sh-shayāṭīn* | **The satans** — negated |
| **221** | *ʿAlā man **tanazzalu**'sh-shayāṭīn* | **The satans** — a question |
| **222** | ***Tanazzalu** ʿalā kulli affākin athīm* | **The satans** — the answer |

**All three occurrences are of the satans.** And the verb used of the revelation is different: *nazala bihi'r-rūḥu'l-amīn* (193) — **Form I.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sūra uses two different forms for two descents. **And eleven verses lie between the two hundred and tenth and the two hundred and twenty-first** — the sūra, that is, takes up the same subject twice, setting a section of commands between them.

### وَمَا يَنۢبَغِى لَهُمْ — "it does not befit"

The root **ب-غ-ي**: **to seek, to want; and to overstep the bound.** Form VII (*inbaghā*) — **to be fitting and proper.**

**The transmitted explanation: the sentence carries both "it is not their business" and "it does not befit them".** It stands, that is, between moral fitness and actual possibility. **And the next clause closes off the possibility separately:** *wa mā yastaṭīʿūn.*

### عَنِ ٱلسَّمْعِ لَمَعْزُولُون — being kept far

The root **ع-ز-ل**: **to set aside, to remove.** *Maʿzūl* — a passive participle: **kept away.**

**And the passive mould must be recorded: they do not keep away of themselves, they *are kept away*.**

**And this subject was treated at `072-cin.md` 72/8-9, at `081-tekvir.md` 81/25 and at `037-saffat.md` 37/6-10, and I rely on those treatments.** The notes there were, in summary, these: the Qurʾān records in several sūras that the sky is "guarded" and that those who attempt to listen are driven off; **this verse is the counterpart of that note in al-Shuʿarāʾ.**

**And in `072-cin.md` al-Shuʿarāʾ 26/210-212 was already cited;** I rely on the note there.

**And the word *al-samʿ* must be recorded: "the hearing".** The same root occurred in several places in the sūra: *innā maʿakum mustamiʿūn* (15), *alā tastamiʿūn* (25), *hal yasmaʿūnakum* (72). **And here it is said that the hearing is *blocked*.**

| Verse | Expression | Who hears / does not hear |
|---|---|---|
| **15** | *Mustamiʿūn* | **God** — listening |
| **25** | *Alā tastamiʿūn* | Pharaoh's listeners |
| **72** | *Hal yasmaʿūnakum* | **The idols** — they do not hear |
| **212** | *ʿAni's-samʿi la-maʿzūlūn* | **The satans** — they cannot reach |
| **223** | *Yulqūna's-samʿa* | **Those who lend them an ear** |

**Five occurrences, one root.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text. **And the sūra builds a line through who hears what.**

### فَلَا تَدْعُ مَعَ ٱللَّهِ إِلَٰهًا ءَاخَرَ — the command turned to the one addressed

**And the verse is one of the most unexpected transitions in the sūra: the address turns directly to the messenger, and a prohibition is set.**

**This is a disputed place. I give the transmitted explanations in a table:**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| **The address is to the messenger, the intent is the community** | A known style in Arabic; there are parallels in the Qurʾān (the sequence at al-Isrāʾ 17/22-39) |
| **The command itself establishes the principle; the state of the addressee is another matter** | The verse lays down a ruling; it is not claimed that the one addressed has broken it |
| **To show that the limit holds for everyone** | The note of punishment that follows (*fa-takūna mina'l-muʿadhdhabīn*) supports this |

**I do not impose a preference.** What is to be recorded is this: **the verse sets down the exceptionlessness of the rule by exemplifying it at the highest station.**

**And the verb *tadʿu* must be recorded: the root د-ع-و — to call, to entreat.** The same root occurred in Abraham's question in the seventy-second verse: *hal yasmaʿūnakum idh **tadʿūn***. **This is a repetition that can be verified from the text and binds the two sections:** Abraham had asked his people "do they hear you when you call?"; **here the one addressed is told "do not call upon another".**

**And the *mina'l-muʿadhdhabīn* mould must be recorded:** one of the last instances of the "assignment to a group" mould that occurs often in the sūra (tabulated at 26/29-31). **And in the hundred and thirty-eighth verse the people of ʿĀd had used the same word:** *wa mā naḥnu bi-**muʿadhdhabīn*** — "we are not going to be punished". **The same word, in two different mouths: one denies it, the other sets it down as a warning.** This is an overlap that can be verified from the text.

---

## 26/214-220 — وَأَنذِرْ عَشِيرَتَكَ ٱلْأَقْرَبِينَ · وَٱخْفِضْ جَنَاحَكَ لِمَنِ ٱتَّبَعَكَ مِنَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ · فَإِنْ عَصَوْكَ فَقُلْ إِنِّى بَرِىٓءٌ مِّمَّا تَعْمَلُونَ · وَتَوَكَّلْ عَلَى ٱلْعَزِيزِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ · ٱلَّذِى يَرَىٰكَ حِينَ تَقُومُ · وَتَقَلُّبَكَ فِى ٱلسَّٰجِدِينَ · إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلسَّمِيعُ ٱلْعَلِيمُ

*Wa andhir ʿashīrataka'l-aqrabīn · Wa'khfiḍ janāḥaka li-mani'ttabaʿaka mina'l-muʾminīn · Fa-in ʿaṣawka fa-qul innī barīʾun mimmā taʿmalūn · Wa tawakkal ʿala'l-ʿazīzi'r-raḥīm · Alladhī yarāka ḥīna taqūm · Wa taqallubaka fi's-sājidīn · Innahu huwa's-samīʿu'l-ʿalīm*

"**And warn your nearest kin. · And lower your wing to those of the believers who follow you. · And if they disobey you, say: 'I am quit of what you do.' · And put your trust in the Mighty, the Merciful — · who sees you when you rise, · and your turning about among those who prostrate. · He indeed is the Hearing, the Knowing.**"

### Seven verses, five commands

| Order | Command | To whom / to what |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Andhir* — warn | **The nearest kin** |
| 2 | *Ikhfiḍ janāḥaka* — lower your wing | **The believers who follow** |
| 3 | *Fa-qul innī barīʾun* — say "I am quit" | **Those who disobey** |
| 4 | *Tawakkal* — trust | **God** |
| — | 218-220 | **The ground** |

**And the order is worth recording: warning → gentleness → separation → trust.** Four commands for four different situations of one task.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the addressees of the four commands:** three commands look to three different groups — **those to be warned, those who follow, those who do not.** The fourth looks to no group; **it orders the messenger's own bearing.**

### عَشِيرَتَكَ ٱلْأَقْرَبِين — the narrowing of the circle

The root **ع-ش-ر**: **ten; and to live together, to mingle.** *ʿAshīra* — **the near community a person lives within, his kin.** From the same root comes *muʿāshara* (living together).
